### Front matter   [ISA-front]

````text
ISA-front-P1
  <1>1 [non] **Date:** 2026-10-29.
  <1>2 [non] **Status:** draft 0.9, **not yet graded**.
  <1>3 [non] It revises draft 0.8, which was **accepted by the council in round 8** (2026-10-28; reports and the editor's responses under `docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/round-{1..8}/` in the `bennett` meta repository) and which remains the contract every other repository targets until this draft is accepted.
  <1>4 [non] §0 lists every change from 0.8.   deps: ISA-0
  <1>5 [non] Implementation is expected to expose frictions; revisions go back through the council.
  <1>6 [non] Founding text of `bennett-isa-spec` (`Bennett-NorthStar.md` §4.4, §6.1); the north star governs where this draft is silent.
  <1>7 [non] The reference interpreter implements 0.8; nothing of this draft is implemented.
  <1>8 [non] MUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD, MAY are normative; *(non-normative)* explains; *(deferred)* names items left open; every deferred decision that an implementer must take carries a **default** in §17, which implementers follow until the item is revised.   deps: ISA-17
ISA-front-P2
  <1>1 [non] **Versioning.** ISA 0.9, object 0.9, launch 0.9, sched 0.9, cert 0.9, coverage 0.9, target 0.9.
  <1>2 [non] The **full version string** (`0.9`) is part of every semantic envelope (§9.2): drafts are not compatible with each other, so every hash of every 0.8 object differs under 0.9 (§0, §16.3).   deps: ISA-9.2, ISA-0, ISA-16.3
````

### §0 Changes from 0.8   [ISA-0]

````text
ISA-0-P1
  <1>1 [non] Every change from the accepted draft 0.8 has one entry below; nothing changes without an entry.
  <1>2 [non] **Kind**: *E* = editorial (wording, or a reading pinned that a conformant 0.8 implementation could already take, with local semantics unchanged); *S* = semantic (some outcome or well-formedness verdict differs from some reading of 0.8, or a capability is added).
  <1>3 [non] **Hash**: every entry rides on the version bump V, which changes envelope part (1) and every signature's `abi`, hence the hash of **every** symbol (§9.2): the four 0.8 example hashes all change and §16.3 republishes their objects (the 0.8 vectors remain valid 0.8 vectors); "+bytes" marks an entry that also changes the canonical bytes of functions that use the affected form; "new" marks new symbols.   deps: ISA-9.2, ISA-16.3
  <1>4 [non] Sources: *EN* = an item of the evidence note `docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md`; *bd* = a bead of this repository; *SQ n* = entry n of `reference-interpreter/SPEC-QUESTIONS.md` (its reading is adopted unless the entry says otherwise); *RC* = the reference-interpreter council round 1 (`docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/round-1/report.md` in `bennett`), member and finding; *r8* = a council round-8 suggestion (bead `b-isa-spec-zsw.N`); *DL* = the dialect 0.2 outline's §25 list.
ISA-0-P2
  <1>1 [non] **No narrowing.** No entry rejects, faults, declares inert or removes a capability that 0.8 permits or the north star promises.
  <1>2 [non] The entries that add or sharpen a well-formedness check (E11's static view rule, E16.9, E17.2, E17.3, E17.5's unmatched uncalls, E17.8, E17.9, E17.10, E17.12, E17.14, the block terminators of K5) concern spellings that always have a legal equivalent or uses that 0.8 did not define soundly (E17.14: a location value as a classical argument would be superposed); the run-time checks (E11, K1's width checks, K3's token validity, K4's inverse-pair check) concern operations that 0.8 did not define or already required (0.8's `qinplace` already demanded its certificates; K4 admits library certificates as well as exhaustive claims, as the north star §3.1 requires).   deps: ISA-0.1-P12.S1 (E11), ISA-0.1-P25.S1 (E16.9), ISA-0.1-P28.S1 (E17.2), ISA-0.1-P29.S1 (E17.3), ISA-0.1-P31.S1 (E17.5), ISA-0.1-P34.S1 (E17.8), ISA-0.1-P35.S1 (E17.9), ISA-0.1-P36.S1 (E17.10), ISA-0.1-P38.S1 (E17.12), ISA-0.1-P40.S1 (E17.14), ISA-0.2-P5.S1 (K5), ISA-0.2-P1.S1 (K1), ISA-0.2-P3.S1 (K3), ISA-0.2-P4.S1 (K4)
````

### §1.1 What the ISA is   [ISA-1.1]

````text
ISA-1.1-P1
  <1>1 [unc] A program model for classical, reversible and coherent computation with one semantics, executed by the VM (`bennett-vm`) and the circuit specialiser (`bennett-circuits`), produced by the plugin (`bennett-plugin`) through the dialect's reference semantics, scheduled through §11, described for hardware by target descriptions (§8).   deps: ISA-11, ISA-8
  <1>2 [req] Nothing here refers to LLVM.
````

### §1.2 Conformance classes   [ISA-1.2]

````text
ISA-1.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] Executor | VM, specialiser, hardware stacks | §3–§7, §14 exactly, and the checking of inverse-pair claims (§4.5, §12.1; a certificate applies when its `closure_hash` binding equals the region's); suites A–E, H   deps: ISA-3, ISA-4, ISA-5, ISA-6, ISA-7, ISA-14, ISA-4.5, ISA-12.1
ISA-1.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] Producer | plugin, library compilers | well-formed objects (§9, §16) and regions (§5, §6); suite F   deps: ISA-9, ISA-16, ISA-5, ISA-6
ISA-1.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] Scheduler | `bennett-sched` and competitors | §11; only schedules valid under §6; suite H   deps: ISA-11, ISA-6
ISA-1.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] Checker | `bennett-conformance` | validates certificates (§12) without trusting producers   deps: ISA-12
ISA-1.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] Target author | vendors | target descriptions (§8); suite E   deps: ISA-8
````

### §1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*   [ISA-1.3]

````text
ISA-1.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of §14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor, accessor and destructor is listed).   deps: ISA-16, ISA-14, ISA-7.3, ISA-10, ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] **Minimality claims** (north star §5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
  <1>3 [req] Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed in §17.   deps: ISA-17
````

### §2 Terminology   [ISA-2]

````text
ISA-2-P1
  <1>1 [def] **Tier.** CC, R, Q.
  <1>2 [def] **Wire**, **bundle** `wire[N]` (little-endian).
ISA-2-P2
  <1>1 [def] **Location** `reg[N]`: a named mutable cell of an oracle or lproc register file.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>2 [def] **Pseudo-location**: an oracle interface input or classical argument, or an lproc classical parameter (§3.7), readable only in its declaring function, never written.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-3.7
ISA-2-P3
  <1>1 [def] **Region**: a Q function with a declared interface: **lifted oracle** (§5.2) or **quantum action** (§5.3); **permutation action** (§5.3).   deps: ISA-5.2, ISA-5.3
ISA-2-P4
  <1>1 [def] **lproc**: a lifted procedure with its own params and locations; it resolves names only to its own params and locations (§3.7).   deps: ISA-3.7
ISA-2-P5
  <1>1 [def] **Budget**: classical (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit).
ISA-2-P6
  <1>1 [def] **Frame**: per machine step: `old_pc`, slots (assignment, capture and delivery slots, §3.6), `flag`, `succ`.   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S4 (delivery slot), ISA-3.6
ISA-2-P7
  <1>1 [def] **Basis configuration**: classical values on every component of D.
ISA-2-P8
  <1>1 [def] **Gate classes.** Permutation: `x cx ccx mcx swap`.
  <1>2 [def] Diagonal: `s sdg t tdg cz rz`.
  <1>3 [def] Non-diagonal: `h rx ry`.
ISA-2-P9
  <1>1 [def] **Verifying executor**: an executor that can decide exactly whether a set of wires is clean on every basis configuration (a state-vector or branch-enumeration simulator); every other executor (hardware) is **non-verifying**.
  <1>2 [req] Rules that depend on the difference say which applies (§3.3 `qreg.free`, §10, §17 item 14).   deps: ISA-3.3, ISA-10, ISA-17-P15
ISA-2-P10
  <1>1 [def] **Effective control context** at a point: the set of wires on the active predicate stack plus the inherited control context (the control wires of every enclosing `ctl` and predicate stack of every enclosing invocation, transitively); duplicates count once.
  <1>2 [def] Its size is the **effective control count**.
````

### §3.1 Tiers and the control invariant   [ISA-3.1]

````text
ISA-3.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] (code block, 4 lines) CC ──calls──▶ R functions (call/uncall) CC ──calls──▶ Q regions   deps: ISA-5.5
ISA-3.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] CC runs on the device's classical-control processor; the host reaches the device only through §10.   deps: ISA-10
  <1>2 [req] **No instruction in any tier makes a control or scheduling decision that depends on the value of a wire** except through predication (§3.5).   deps: ISA-3.5
  <1>3 [req] Gate angles are finite `f64` literals or classical arguments fixed at region entry.
ISA-3.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Call-tier policy** (suite F). The diagram, completed with same-tier calls; a call, uncall or region call outside this table is ill-formed:   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed)
ISA-3.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] CC function | CC and R functions | of a CC or R callee | oracles, actions | –
ISA-3.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] R function | R functions | of an R callee | oracles, actions | –
ISA-3.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] oracle body, lproc | – | – | – | `lift.call` of lprocs; `lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall` of oracles; `lift.perm_call` of permutation actions   deps: ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-2-P3.S1 (permutation action)
ISA-3.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] action | – | – | oracles, actions (§5.3, §5.5) | –   deps: ISA-5.3, ISA-5.5
ISA-3.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] An R function never calls a CC function: CC code may measure and make host requests (§4.1), which an R history cannot reverse.   deps: ISA-4.1
````

### §3.2 State   [ISA-3.2]

````text
ISA-3.2-P1
  <1>1 [def] classical environment | CC, R | dynamic SSA instances (§7.4), block parameters, activation stack, **quantum-register handles** (`qreg[n]`, `qreg[*]`, §3.3), tokens (§5.4)   deps: ISA-7.4, ISA-3.3, ISA-5.4
ISA-3.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] register and token ledgers | CC, R | every register's id, class, width and state (§3.3); every device-minted token's tuple and validity (§5.4)   deps: ISA-3.3, ISA-5.4
ISA-3.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] classical memory | CC, R | byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse; allocation table (§7.1)   deps: ISA-7.1
ISA-3.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] effect tape, entropy tape | R | §7.3   deps: ISA-7.3
ISA-3.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] run history | CC, R | one frame per executed instruction (§7.4)   deps: ISA-7.4
ISA-3.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] wire store | Q (CC and R via `qreg`) | live wires with roles `input target fault borrowed mutable workspace location cell`
ISA-3.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] quantum memory | Q | regions of `wire[W]` cells: local (§4.6) or interface (§5.1)   deps: ISA-4.6, ISA-5.1
ISA-3.2-P8
  <1>1 [req] register file | Q | locations of the active oracle and its lproc closure (§3.7)   deps: ISA-3.6-P1.S1 (lproc closure), ISA-3.7
ISA-3.2-P9
  <1>1 [req] predicate stack | Q | wires; the active predicate is their conjunction
ISA-3.2-P10
  <1>1 [req] oracle step state | Q | `pc, pc' : wire[P]`; `faulted : wire`; `code : wire[F]`; `result_val : wire[T]`; counters (§3.7); B frames   deps: ISA-3.7
ISA-3.2-P11
  <1>1 [req] budget | Q | classical: B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit
````

### §3.3 Types, ownership, operand schema   [ISA-3.3]

````text
ISA-3.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] (code block, 5 lines) iN (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) ptr (width ptr_width) f64 token qreg[n] qreg[*] region[A,W]
ISA-3.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Widths.** The bound 1 ≤ N ≤ 128 applies to the type `iN` only (a lifted row's `.iN` suffix names a location width, §4.2).   deps: ISA-4.2
  <1>2 [req] `wire[N]`, `reg[N]` and `qreg[n]` have N, n ≥ 1 and `region[A,W]` has A ≥ 0, W ≥ 1, with no upper bound (a `qreg[*]` register's width is a run-time value n ≥ 0, §3.3 below); a value of these types is a bit-vector of its width in every semantics (§14.1), including interface bundles, pseudo-locations, locations, cells, stack cells and frame slots.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle), ISA-3.3, ISA-14.1
ISA-3.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Linear SSA with implicit versioning** (structured code and CC region calls): a `wire`/`wire[N]` value is produced once and consumed once; mode `in` consumes and re-produces a successor of the same name; `consume` consumes; `produce` creates; a name is not used after `consume`.
  <1>2 [req] **Regions are threaded the same way.**   deps: ISA-2-P3.S1 (Region)
  <1>3 [def] **Indexed operand** `w[i]` (literal i) borrows wire i for one instruction.
ISA-3.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Disjointness.** The wire operands of one instruction are pairwise distinct; **every wire of the effective control context is distinct from every target and from every consumed or produced operand** of an instruction executed under it (so `qpred p; x p` is ill-formed); a predicate wire is not modified between its push and its matching pop; a `qif`/`qloop … while` selector is not modified inside its arms or body.   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed)
  <1>2 [req] A region's interface bundles and regions are pairwise disjoint.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>3 [req] Distinctness is judged on the wires denoted: a bundle and an indexed operand of it, or two indexed operands denoting the same wire, overlap and are not distinct.   deps: ~ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)   declared: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.4
ISA-3.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Per-opcode operand schema** (§4.8): every opcode has a tier set, a fixed arity, operand kinds and a result type; results are written first (`w = op a b`), modes are never written, and **result types that are not determined by the operands are written as a suffix** (`r = const.i32 5`, `r = zext.i64 a`, `r = trunc.i8 a`, `r = ptrtoint.i64 p`, `p = inttoptr a`).   deps: ISA-4.8
  <1>2 [req] Width rules: `binop`/`icmp` operands equal width; `select` arms equal width; `zext`/`sext` strictly widen, `trunc` strictly narrows; `load.iN` and `lift.load.iN` require N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}; `measure` of `qreg[n]` with n ≤ 128 yields `in`, and `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) of a `qreg[*]` yields `iN`; signed division truncates toward zero.
  <1>3 [req] The literal c of `const.iN c` and `lift.const.iN c` satisfies −2^(N−1) ≤ c < 2^N and denotes c mod 2^N (both the signed and the unsigned spelling of every value are accepted); any other literal is ill-formed (suite F).   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed)
ISA-3.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Lifted width rules** (suite F; a mismatch is ill-formed, never a truncation or an extension). Every address operand (`a` of `lift.load`/ `lift.store`, `p` of `lift.boundscheck`/`lift.free`) and every address result (`w` of `lift.addr`/`lift.alloc`) is `reg[ptr_width]`; the length `n` of `lift.alloc`/`lift.boundscheck` is a location of any width or a literal in [0, 2^ptr_width); `inrange` and every prospective sum are computed at width max(widths of the operands involved) + 1 with no wrap.   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed)
  <1>2 [req] The destination of `lift.label` and the operand of `qjumpr` have any width N with 2^N > n (the index-space size).
  <1>3 [req] `lift.call` location arguments have exactly the callee's parameter widths and its destination the callee's result width; its token, classical and region lists match the callee's corresponding groups in count, order and type (§3.7); `lift.ret v` has the containing lproc's result width; `lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`/`lift.perm_call` operands have exactly the callee signature widths, and `mem` operands name caller regions whose `[A,W]` equal the callee's and whose role is `in` for an `in` slot (an `in` region is never passed to a `mut` slot); `fault C` and `lift.faultif p C` require the literal C < 2^F, where F is the fault width of the containing oracle or, in an lproc, of every oracle whose lproc closure contains it (checked at load and again at link; an lproc in no oracle's closure is checked when linked into one); the width rules above for `binop`, `icmp`, `select`, `zext`, `sext`, `trunc` apply verbatim to their `lift.*` counterparts; the destinations of one `lift.parmov` are pairwise distinct.   deps: ISA-3.7-P18.S1 (`lift.ret v`), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-3.6-P1.S1 (lproc closure), ISA-3.7
ISA-3.3-P7
  <1>1 [req] **Quantum registers in CC and R.** A **register** is n device-resident wires of one class with a nonzero 64-bit **id**, recorded in the program's **register ledger** with its class, width and state: **live**, **viewed** (one slice of it is live) or **consumed**.
  <1>2 [req] `qreg[n]` is the type of a register whose width n is fixed statically; `qreg[*]` the type of a register whose width is known only at run time (an unsigned 64-bit n ≥ 0).
  <1>3 [req] A `qreg[n]` value may be used wherever a `qreg[*]` is expected; a `qreg[*]` value bound to a `qreg[n]` parameter or block parameter (`call`, `br`, `ret`, launch) or to a slot of width n (region call, §4.7) is checked at that transfer: its width MUST equal the required one.   deps: ISA-4.7
  <1>4 [req] The `qreg.*` instructions execute in CC and R; `measure` only in CC.
  <1>5 [req] An instruction whose precondition fails is a CC trap / R fault (§4.9) with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` unless the table says otherwise, and has no effect.   deps: ISA-4.9
  <1>6 [req] An id's value is chosen by the executor (the suites compare ids only for equality); an id is never reassigned to another register while the program is loaded.
  <1>7 [def] **Handle transitions:**
ISA-3.3-P8
  <1>1 [req] `q = qreg.new cls n` | `n` a literal n ≥ 1 (q: `qreg[n]`) or an `i64` value read unsigned, 0 included (q: `qreg[*]`); the device can provide n wires of class `cls`, else code `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | q live, clean, with a fresh id and wires disjoint from every live or viewed register.   declared: ISA-3.3-P7
ISA-3.3-P9
  <1>1 [req] `qreg.free q` | q live, not a view, clean on every basis configuration (a verifying executor checks it; on failure q stays live and unchanged; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14) | q consumed   deps: ISA-2-P9.S1 (non-verifying), ISA-17-P15
ISA-3.3-P10
  <1>1 [req] `q2 = qreg.slice q lo len` | q live, not a view; `lo` and `len` each a literal (`len` ≥ 1) or an `i64` value, both unsigned; lo + len ≤ n, computed without wrap (a trap even when literals make the failure evident) | q2 a view of wires [lo, lo+len) of q, of type `qreg[len]` for a literal len and `qreg[*]` otherwise; q viewed; q2 MUST be the operand of exactly one region call and of nothing else; after that call q2 is consumed and q is live again; a use of q while it is viewed traps (the static rule below covers the cases suite F rejects).
  <1>2 [req] Reversing that region call (§7.4) restores q viewed and q2 live; reversing the slice frame then restores q live   deps: ISA-7.4
ISA-3.3-P11
  <1>1 [req] `q = qreg.concat q1 q2` | q1, q2 live, not views, distinct registers | q1, q2 consumed; q live, with a fresh id, over the wires of q1 then q2; `qreg[n1+n2]` if both operands are static, else `qreg[*]`
ISA-3.3-P12
  <1>1 [req] `w = qreg.width q` | q live, not a view | w: `i64`, the width; no state change
ISA-3.3-P13
  <1>1 [req] `id = qreg.id q` | q live, not a view | id: `i64`, the bits of q's id; no state change
ISA-3.3-P14
  <1>1 [req] `q = qreg.from_id id` | `id` is the id of a register of the program that is live, not a view, and not owned by another run that is not CLOSED (§10) | q: `qreg[*]`, an alias of that register; no ledger state change; the run now owns the register (§10), which reversal does not undo (ownership ends when the run is CLOSED)   deps: ISA-10
ISA-3.3-P15
  <1>1 [req] region call (§4.7) with q as operand | q live (a view included); its width equals the slot's (§3.3 binding); the wire sets filling distinct wire/region slots are pairwise disjoint, and an explicit control is disjoint from targets and consumed/produced operands (read-only input overlap remains allowed), else CC trap / R fault FAULT_UB_TRAP before any effect, including any view consumption.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap), ISA-4.7, ~ISA-3.3   declared: ISA-3.3-P4, ISA-4.7, ISA-4.9
  <1>2 [req] | q unchanged.   declared: ISA-4.7
ISA-3.3-P16
  <1>1 [req] `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | q live, not a view; the form `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) for a `qreg[*]`, whose width MUST be N (checked before measuring); for a `qreg[n]` either `measure` or `measure.iN` with N = n (checked statically) | q consumed, its wires released; r: `in` / `iN`
ISA-3.3-P17
  <1>1 [req] reversal of a `qreg.*` frame (§7.4, §10) | – | the ledger state before that frame (below).   deps: ISA-7.4, ISA-10
  <1>2 [req] A `measure` frame is **never reversed**: a segment containing one cannot be uncalled (§7.4) and a run containing one is not reversible (§10)   deps: ISA-7.4, ISA-10
ISA-3.3-P18
  <1>1 [req] **Ledger frames and their reversal** (§7.4). A `qreg.new` frame records the id, class and width; its reversal releases that register without a test: every later frame touching it has been undone first (LIFO) and the run owns it exclusively (§10), so under T4's exactness hypothesis it is clean, and otherwise its residual, within the declared bounds of the undone realisations (§12.3), is discarded with it.   deps: ISA-14.3-P4.S1 (T4), ISA-5.5-P1.S5 (LIFO), ISA-7.4, ISA-10, ISA-12.3
  <1>2 [req] A `qreg.free` frame records the id, class, width and whether its cleanliness was verified; its reversal re-creates the register clean and live with the same id, class and width (every alias sees it live again).
  <1>3 [req] A `qreg.slice` frame records the parent id, lo and len, a `qreg.concat` frame the two operand ids and the new id; their reversal restores the exact prior states and wire order without moving, copying or measuring a wire.
  <1>4 [req] `qreg.width`, `qreg.id` and `qreg.from_id` change no ledger state and their frames are pure (§7.4); if an `uncall` later removes the register an alias refers to, every use of the alias traps.   deps: ISA-7.4
ISA-3.3-P19
  <1>1 [req] **Binding**: a register of width n (`qreg[n]`, or `qreg[*]` of run-time width n) fills a `wire[n]` slot bit for bit (bit 0 ↔ wire 0); it fills a `region[A,W]` slot iff n = 2^A·W with cell i = bits [iW, (i+1)W); for a `qreg[*]` the width is checked at the call, before any effect.
  <1>2 [req] Host-supplied register ids are `qreg` values of the parameter's type.
ISA-3.3-P20
  <1>1 [req] The register ids supplied as arguments of one `bennett_run` MUST denote pairwise disjoint registers, else `bennett_run` returns `E_ARG` before executing the entry function or acquiring any register ownership.   declared: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-10
ISA-3.3-P21
  <1>1 [req] **Registers, handles and aliases.** A `qreg` value is a reference to a register of the device; the state live / viewed / consumed belongs to the register, not to a name.
  <1>2 [req] `qreg` values pass unchanged as `call` arguments, `ret` values and `br` block arguments (each transfer binds another name to the same register and changes no state; a `qreg[*]` reaching a `qreg[n]` parameter is width-checked, above).
  <1>3 [req] Two names bound to one register (by such transfers or by `qreg.from_id`) are **aliases**: a transition made through either is observed through both.
  <1>4 [req] The wire operands of one region call, with its effective control context, MUST denote pairwise disjoint wires (§3.3 disjointness); aliases violate it.   deps: ISA-3.3
  <1>5 [req] **Static and run-time verdicts.** Suite F rejects exactly: two `qreg` operands of one region call or of one `qreg.concat` that are the same name; and, on some path from a `qreg.slice`, a use of the view's name other than as the operand of one region call (a `br`, `call` or `ret` transfer included), its reaching a `ret` or the end of the function unconsumed, or a use of the slice's parent operand name before that call.
  <1>6 [req] Every other overlap, and every use of a viewed register through another name, is a CC trap / R fault with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap)
````

### §3.4 Execution in CC and R   [ISA-3.4]

````text
ISA-3.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] Sequential small-step execution with an instruction pointer, block parameters instead of phi nodes, dynamic control flow and allocation; every executed instruction pushes a run-history frame (§7.4).   deps: ISA-7.4
ISA-3.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Names and definitions.** Every name used in a CC or R function is a parameter, a block parameter or the result of at least one defining instruction of that function, and all its definitions give it one type (suite F).
  <1>2 [req] The text order of blocks is irrelevant: a definition need not precede its uses textually.
  <1>3 [req] Executing a use of a name that has no current instance (none was created on the executed path, or an `uncall` removed it, §7.4) is a CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap), ISA-7.4
  <1>4 [req] Every CC or R block ends in `br`, `ret`, `trap` or `fault` (suite F); `trap` and `fault` end the run (§4.9).   deps: ISA-4.9
````

### §3.5 Predication   [ISA-3.5]

````text
ISA-3.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] Every Q instruction with unitary semantics executes controlled on the active predicate.
  <1>2 [req] `qpred w` pushes, `qunpred w` pops the same wire; the stack is empty at region entry and exit.
  <1>3 [req] Allocation, borrowing, release, region creation and image gates are **unconditional**; only gates (including those inside copy/XOR/exchange/memory instructions) are controlled.
````

### §3.6 The predicated step machine   [ISA-3.6]

````text
ISA-3.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] **Index space.** `I[0..n)` = the **rows** (one index per instruction line) of the oracle's blocks, blocks in text order and rows in text order within each block; then the rows of every lproc in its **lproc closure** (transitively reachable through `lift.call`, linked ones included), placed as follows: partition the closure into its SCCs (§9.2); a one-member SCC's **order key** is its member's closure hash, a multi-member SCC's order key is its closure `scc_hash`; SCCs are placed in increasing bytewise order of their keys (64-hex), the members of a multi-member SCC consecutively in SCC index order; each lproc's binding instances (§3.7) consecutively in binding-key order, each instance's rows in block text order as for the oracle.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-9.2-P3.S13 (SCCs), ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash), ISA-9.2, ISA-3.7
  <1>2 [req] The oracle and each lproc instance therefore occupy a contiguous range of indices, and a **label `^b` denotes the index of the first row of block b**.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>3 [def] `HALT = n`; 2^P > n.
  <1>4 [req] Branch and call targets are labels; `w = lift.label ^b` is the only way a location obtains an index value; executors MAY re-index dispatch extensionally **provided every label value observable through `lift.label` keeps its canonical numeric value and a `qjumpr` to any in-range index reaches the row that canonical index names**.
  <1>5 [req] **Terminators**: `qjump qbranch qjumpr lift. ret lift.retval fault`; every block ends in one; no label names HALT; **only `lift.retval` reaches HALT with success**.
ISA-3.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Row classes.** Each §4.2 row is an **assignment** (A: written locations/cells receive values computed out of place into frame slots `disp_j`, then exchanged in, so `disp_j` retains the displaced old value), a **direct** row (D: a controlled permutation in place), or **mixed** (A+D) with an explicit ordered list of **elementary sub-operations** printed in §3.7.   deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-3.7
  <1>2 [req] **Slot kinds.** An **assignment slot** receives a value computed out of place and is then exchanged with its destination (it retains the displaced old value).
  <1>3 [req] A **capture slot** receives a value computed out of place and is never exchanged (it retains that value as history: the arguments of `lift.call`).
  <1>4 [def] The **delivery slot** of `lift.ret` is a capture slot that is subsequently exchanged, under a condition, with the destination location.   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S3 (capture slot)
  <1>5 [req] Rows with a **flag** compute it into `frame.flag` from the old state before any effect; rows with a data-dependent successor compute it into `frame. succ` before any effect; conditional-fault rows perform `faulted ^= flag`, `code ^= flag·C′` after their other effects, where C′ is the row's **effective cause** (§5.4: C′ = C, except C′ = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` when a `lift.faultif` literal C is 0).   deps: ISA-5.4
ISA-3.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Frame layout** (fixed per oracle): `(old_pc : wire[P], disp_1..disp_ {k_max}, flag : wire, succ : wire[P])`, `k_max` and widths `W_j` from the widest write sets used (§4.2 lists each row's slot use).   deps: ISA-4.2
ISA-3.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Forward machine step** t (1 ≤ t ≤ B), reads referring to the state at the start of the step:
ISA-3.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Dispatch.** `at_k = (pc == k)` for k = 0..n into clean scratch.
ISA-3.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Execute.** For each k < n in index order: push `at_k`; run `I[k]`'s row: (a) compute `flag` into `frame_t.flag` if the row has one; (b) compute `succ` into `frame_t.succ` if data-dependent; (c) for A rows, compute **every** written value into its slot, then exchange **every** slot with its destination (all computes precede all exchanges); for D rows apply the row's permutation; for A+D rows follow the row's listed order; (d) apply the fault writes; pop `at_k`.   deps: ISA-3.6-P16.S4 (A+D row)
    <2>1 [req] (a) compute `flag` into `frame_t.flag` if the row has one
    <2>2 [req] (b) compute `succ` into `frame_t.succ` if data-dependent
    <2>3 [req] (c) for A rows, compute **every** written value into its slot, then exchange **every** slot with its destination (all computes precede all exchanges); for D rows apply the row's permutation; for A+D rows follow the row's listed order   deps: ISA-3.6-P16.S2 (D row), ISA-3.6-P16.S4 (A+D row)
    <2>4 [req] (d) apply the fault writes; pop `at_k`.
ISA-3.6-P7
  <1>1 [req] **Successor.** For each k < n, under `at_k`: XOR the row's successor into `pc'` (a label index under `flag`/`¬flag` as stated, or `frame_t.succ`).
  <1>2 [req] Under `at_HALT`: XOR `HALT` into `pc'`.
ISA-3.6-P8
  <1>1 [req] **Commit pc.** `qexch pc pc'`; `qexch pc' frame_t.old_pc` (now `pc` is the next index, `pc'` is clean, `old_pc` holds the old index).
ISA-3.6-P9
  <1>1 [req] **Clean dispatch.** Uncompute every `at_k` from `frame_t.old_pc`.
ISA-3.6-P10
  <1>1 [req] Decrement the classical step counter.
ISA-3.6-P11
  <1>1 [req] On an **idle** step (pc = HALT at its start) no row executes, step 3 XORs `HALT` into `pc'`, and step 4 leaves pc = HALT and `frame_t.old_pc` = HALT; the inverse step clears it.
  <1>2 [req] (A variant that leaves idle frames at zero is deferred, §17 item 17.)   deps: ISA-17-P18
ISA-3.6-P12
  <1>1 [def] **Inverse machine step** (prefix reversal runs it for t = B..1), in this order:   deps: ISA-5.2-P3.S1 (prefix reversal)
ISA-3.6-P13
  <1>1 [def] Recompute every `at_k` from `frame_t.old_pc`.
ISA-3.6-P14
  <1>1 [req] `qexch pc' frame_t.old_pc`; `qexch pc pc'` (restores the old `pc`; `pc'` again holds the successor value; `old_pc` is clean).
ISA-3.6-P15
  <1>1 [req] For each k < n under `at_k`: XOR the row's successor into `pc'` again, using `frame_t.flag` / `frame_t.succ`; under `at_HALT`: XOR `HALT` into `pc'` (`pc'` is now clean).
ISA-3.6-P16
  <1>1 [req] For each k < n in **reverse index order** under `at_k`: undo the fault writes (`faulted ^= flag`, `code ^= flag·C′`); then undo the row's effects.
  <1>2 [req] **D row**: its sub-operations in reverse order, each by its own inverse (`lift.perm_call` by the callee's adjoint; XORs, controlled flips and exchanges by themselves).
  <1>3 [req] **A row**: exchange every assignment slot back with its destination **in the reverse of the forward exchange order** (the forward order is the row's listed slot order; every destination address, forward and inverse, is evaluated from the state current at that exchange, so an indexed destination such as `alloctab[atop]` is addressed with the counter value that the earlier inverse exchanges have already restored), then **uncompute every slot from the restored old state** (the same out-of-place computation, applied again as XOR, clears it).   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S2 (assignment slot)
  <1>4 [req] **A+D row**: **the exact reverse of its complete §3.7 sub-operation list**, each elementary sub-operation inverted at its reversed position: a direct sub-operation by its own inverse; an assignment sub-operation by exchanging its slot back and then uncomputing the slot; a capture sub-operation by uncomputing the slot (its source has been restored by the sub-operations already undone); the delivery sub-operation by the same conditional exchanges.   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>5 [req] **Every address and condition is evaluated from the state current at that position of the inverse** (so the `lift.ret` stack addresses and delivery condition use the callee `fp` restored one position earlier, and the `lift.call` record base uses the restored `sp`); §3.7 prints both inverse lists.   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>6 [req] Finally uncompute `frame_t.succ` and `frame_t.flag` from the restored old state.
ISA-3.6-P17
  <1>1 [req] Uncompute every `at_k` from `pc` (which now equals the old index).
ISA-3.6-P18
  <1>1 [req] After the inverse step every frame field is clean.
  <1>2 [non] *(non-normative)* The forward step is a permutation Π_t; the inverse step is Π_t⁻¹ written out; T2 relies on nothing else.   deps: ISA-14.3-P2.S1 (T2)
  <1>3 [req] B = 0 is legal: the oracle commits `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` on every input.
````

### §3.7 Register files, procedures, counters, stack   [ISA-3.7]

````text
ISA-3.7-P1
  <1>1 [req] **Name resolution.** A read operand of a lifted row in an **oracle body** is a location of the oracle or a pseudo-location (an interface input or a classical argument).
  <1>2 [req] A read operand in an **lproc** is a param or location of that lproc; nothing else.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>3 [req] Token and region parameters are usable only in their operand kinds below, not as bit-vector locations.
  <1>4 [req] A written operand is a location of the containing function.
  <1>5 [req] Globals and mapped interface regions are reached only by address: `w = lift.addr @g`.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region)
  <1>6 [req] Lprocs receive everything else as parameters.
ISA-3.7-P2
  <1>1 [req] An oracle declares `regs { … }`; an lproc declares five header groups:   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>2 [req] (code block, 3 lines) lproc @f (params..) (result) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..)
ISA-3.7-P3
  <1>1 [req] The first group consists of `reg[N]` parameters, the second of exactly one `reg[T]` result location; the third consists of `token` parameters, the fourth of `iN`, `ptr` or `f64` parameters, the fifth of `region[A,W]` parameters with role `in`.
  <1>2 [req] Token and classical parameters are immutable; their optional role suffixes are `token` and `classical` respectively and MUST agree with their group (§16.2).   deps: ISA-16.2
  <1>3 [req] They are not locations, stack cells or frame slots.
  <1>4 [req] Classical parameters can be read as pseudo-locations (§4.7); tokens cannot be read as bits or constructed from them.   deps: ISA-4.7
  <1>5 [req] Region parameters name the passed mapped regions, not copies of their cells.   deps: ISA-2-P3.S1 (Region)
  <1>6 [req] Names in all groups and `regs` are distinct.
ISA-3.7-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Argument binding.** The complete call form is `w = lift.call @f (a_1..a_m) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..)`.
  <1>2 [req] The first list supplies the location parameters using the capture and bind steps below.
  <1>3 [req] The token and classical lists supply parameters from the calling oracle's `args`, or from the calling lproc's token and classical parameters, with exactly matching types.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>4 [req] A location (including a `reg[N]` parameter), an input wire or a literal cannot supply either list.
  <1>5 [req] The region list supplies mapped interface regions of the calling oracle, or region parameters of the calling lproc, with matching `[A,W]` and `in` role.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>6 [req] Thus the enclosing oracle passes all three lists explicitly; a recursive or other lproc call forwards them explicitly.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>7 [req] The original form `w = lift.call @f (a_1..a_m)` is canonical exactly when all three additional groups are empty; otherwise all four lists are required, including empty ones.
ISA-3.7-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Fixed binding instances.** For one oracle invocation, resolve those three lists transitively to the oracle's entry arguments and mapped regions.
  <1>2 [req] An instance is a pair (lproc, binding key); its key is the concatenation of the zero-based oracle `args` positions supplying its token parameters, then its classical parameters, then the zero-based oracle `mem` positions supplying its region parameters.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>3 [req] Compare keys lexicographically as integer sequences.
  <1>4 [req] Starting from calls in the oracle body, close this set under calls in every reached instance; only finitely many keys exist, even with recursion, since no call constructs new token, classical or region bindings.   deps: ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body)
  <1>5 [req] Distinct keys stay distinct even when entry values are equal.
  <1>6 [req] An lproc with no such parameters has the single empty key, preserving its previous indexing.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
ISA-3.7-P6
  <1>1 [req] The index space (§3.6), locations and labels are instantiated per key.   deps: ISA-3.6
  <1>2 [req] A call targets the callee instance selected by its resolved key; labels and `qjumpr` ranges are local to the containing instance.
  <1>3 [req] All instance bindings are classical and fixed before oracle entry; reaching a call under a wire predicate only predicates its existing row operations.
  <1>4 [req] No classical binding changes as a result of quantum control.
  <1>5 [req] Different coherent paths may call the same lproc with different bindings: they reach distinct instances, each using its own fixed arguments.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>6 [req] This is the reference semantics; executors MAY share their storage or inline instances only extensionally (§5.7).   deps: ISA-5.7
ISA-3.7-P7
  <1>1 [req] Each instance declares its own `regs`.
  <1>2 [req] All locations are clean at entry.
  <1>3 [req] The oracle's `result_val : wire[T]` (T = the `tgt` width) is step state, initially 0.
ISA-3.7-P8
  <1>1 [req] **Counters and sizes.** Size exponents: `mem` 2^H byte cells, `alloctab` 2^A_tab entries, stack 2^S_cells cells.
  <1>2 [req] Counter widths: `heap_used : wire[Hc]`, Hc ≥ H + 2; `atop : wire[Ac]`, Ac ≥ A_tab + 1; `sp, fp : wire[Sc]`, Sc ≥ S_cells + 1; `depth : wire[Dc]`, Dc ≥ ⌈log₂(depth_limit + 2)⌉.
  <1>3 [req] Every prospective sum (`heap_used + max(n,1)`, `sp + R_f`, `depth + 1`) is computed at width max(operand widths) + 1 before comparison, with no wrap.
  <1>4 [req] `bennett_budget` rejects limits that do not fit (§5.4).   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>5 [req] The layout fixes no counter width: the reference realisation uses the minimum widths Hc = H + 2, Ac = A_tab + 1, Sc = S_cells + 1 and Dc = ⌈log₂(depth_limit + 2)⌉ (Dc from the invocation's budget).
  <1>6 [req] Every reachable counter value fits them (heap_used ≤ heap_limit ≤ heap_limit_max < 2^H, atop ≤ 2^A_tab, fp ≤ sp ≤ stack_limit ≤ 2^S_cells, depth ≤ depth_limit ≤ 2^Dc − 2, each counter advancing only when its flag is clear), so an executor MAY use wider counters with the same F_B (§5.7); `W_cell` below uses the reference Sc.   deps: ISA-5.7
ISA-3.7-P9
  <1>1 [def] **Initial allocator state** (oracle entry): `heap_used = 0`, bump = `heap_base`, `atop` = number of pre-entries, `sp = fp = 0`, `depth = 0`, `alloctab[0..atop)` = one pinned live entry per global in `layout. globals` order then per mapped interface region in `layout.interface_ regions` order, storage of `mem` all zero except global images.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region)
ISA-3.7-P10
  <1>1 [req] **Locations and the result name.** Locations of a function are numbered in declaration order: an oracle's `regs`; for an lproc instance its `reg[N]` parameters, then its **result name** (`(r:reg[8])` declares a location `r` of the lproc, written like any location and read by `lift.ret`), then its `regs`.   deps: ISA-2-P2.S1 (Location), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>2 [req] Every location of the oracle and its instantiated closure has a global id (Did bits: index-space order of instances, then declaration order).
  <1>3 [def] `L_f` = all locations of the selected instance of `@f`, m′ = |L_f| (`reg[N]` params + 1 + regs for an lproc); token, classical and region parameters contribute no locations or stack cells; **R_f = 3 + m′** cells; cell width W_cell = max(P, Sc, Did, W_max) with W_max the widest location width in the closure.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>4 [def] A location of width N exchanged with a cell exchanges the cell's low N bits; the cell's remaining bits stay clean.
ISA-3.7-P11
  <1>1 [req] **`w = lift.call @f (a_1..a_m) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..)`.** Resolve the fixed callee instance above; m counts only location arguments. flag := (depth + 1 > depth_limit) ∨ (sp + R_f > stack_limit), C = `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH`, computed first into `frame.flag`; the elementary sub-operations C1–C5, in this order, run under ¬flag:   deps: ISA-3.7-P12.S1 (C1), ISA-3.7-P13.S1 (C2), ISA-3.7-P14.S1 (C3), ISA-3.7-P15.S1 (C4), ISA-3.7-P16.S1 (C5), ISA-3.6-P2.S1 (elementary sub-operations)
ISA-3.7-P12
  <1>1 [def] **C1 capture**: `disp_i ^= a_i` for i = 1..m (capture slots; every `a_i` is read where it is, before any location moves);   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S3 (capture slot)
ISA-3.7-P13
  <1>1 [def] **C2 push locations**: exchange `L_f` (each padded to one cell) with the clean cells `stack[sp+3 .. sp+3+m′)`;
ISA-3.7-P14
  <1>1 [def] **C3 record**: `stack[sp] ^= k+1` (ret index), `stack[sp+1] ^= fp`, `stack[sp+2] ^= id(w)` (direct XORs into clean cells);
ISA-3.7-P15
  <1>1 [def] **C4 bind**: `param_i(@f) ^= disp_i` for i = 1..m (the params are among L_f and clean after C2);   deps: ISA-3.7-P13.S1 (C2)
ISA-3.7-P16
  <1>1 [def] **C5 counters** (assignment): `disp_{m+1} ^= sp`, `disp_{m+2} ^= sp + R_f`, `disp_{m+3} ^= depth + 1` (all computed from the old counters), then `qexch disp_{m+1} fp`, `qexch disp_{m+2} sp`, `qexch disp_{m+3} depth` (now `fp` = old `sp`; the slots hold the old `fp`, `sp`, `depth`).
ISA-3.7-P17
  <1>1 [req] Successor: the entry label of the selected instance of `@f`.
  <1>2 [req] **Inverse** (§3.6 step 4, after the fault-write undo): C5⁻¹ `qexch disp_{m+3} depth; qexch disp_{m+2} sp; qexch disp_{m+1} fp` (the old counters are back), then `disp_{m+3} ^= depth + 1; disp_{m+2} ^= sp + R_f; disp_{m+1} ^= sp` (slots clean); C4⁻¹ `param_i ^= disp_i`; C3⁻¹ `stack[sp+2] ^= id(w); stack[sp+1] ^= fp; stack[sp] ^= k+1` at the restored `sp`, `fp`; C2⁻¹ exchange `L_f` back with `stack[sp+3 .. sp+3+m′)`; C1⁻¹ `disp_i ^= a_i` (the `a_i` are restored by C2⁻¹ even when they are locations of `@f` itself, as in a recursive call).   deps: ISA-3.7-P16.S1 (C5), ISA-3.7-P15.S1 (C4), ISA-3.7-P14.S1 (C3), ISA-3.7-P13.S1 (C2), ISA-3.7-P12.S1 (C1), ISA-3.6
ISA-3.7-P18
  <1>1 [def] **`lift.ret v`** (in an lproc `@f`; flag := (depth = 0), C = `FAULT_UB_ TRAP`; R1–R5 run under ¬flag):   deps: ISA-3.7-P19.S1 (R1), ISA-3.7-P20.S1 (R2), ISA-3.7-P21.S1 (R3), ISA-3.7-P22.S1 (R4), ISA-3.7-P23.S1 (R5), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
ISA-3.7-P19
  <1>1 [def] **R1 succ**: `frame.succ ^= stack[fp]` (ret index);
ISA-3.7-P20
  <1>1 [def] **R2 capture result**: `disp_1 ^= v` (delivery slot);   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S4 (delivery slot)
ISA-3.7-P21
  <1>1 [def] **R3 pop locations**: exchange `L_f` with `stack[fp+3 .. fp+3+m′)` (the locations regain the caller's view; the cells hold the callee's final values as history);
ISA-3.7-P22
  <1>1 [def] **R4 deliver**: for every location ℓ of the closure, under `(stack[fp+2] == id(ℓ))`: `qexch disp_1 ℓ` (exactly one condition holds; the slot retains ℓ's old value);
ISA-3.7-P23
  <1>1 [def] **R5 counters** (assignment): `disp_2 ^= stack[fp+1]`, `disp_3 ^= depth − 1`, then `qexch disp_2 fp`, `qexch disp_3 depth`.
ISA-3.7-P24
  <1>1 [req] Successor: `frame.succ`.
  <1>2 [req] **Inverse**: R5⁻¹ `qexch disp_3 depth; qexch disp_2 fp` (the callee `fp` is back), then `disp_3 ^= depth − 1; disp_2 ^= stack[fp+1]` (slots clean); R4⁻¹ the same conditional exchanges, their condition and addresses at the restored `fp` (ℓ regains its old value, `disp_1` again holds v); R3⁻¹ exchange `L_f` back with `stack[fp+3 .. fp+3+m′)` (the callee's values are back); R2⁻¹ `disp_1 ^= v`; R1⁻¹ `frame. succ ^= stack[fp]` (this is the generic `succ` uncompute that ends §3.6 step 4).   deps: ISA-3.7-P23.S1 (R5), ISA-3.7-P22.S1 (R4), ISA-3.7-P21.S1 (R3), ISA-3.7-P20.S1 (R2), ISA-3.7-P19.S1 (R1), ISA-3.6
ISA-3.7-P25
  <1>1 [req] The stack is monotonic (`sp` only grows; records are history).
  <1>2 [req] Callee rows are charged to the caller's budget; `lift.call` and `lift.ret` are one step each.
  <1>3 [req] Forwarding or resolving the fixed token, classical and region bindings adds no rows, capture slots or stack cells.   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S3 (capture slot)
  <1>4 [req] A nested `lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall` is one outer row and executes the callee with its own token's tuple; its internal rows do not decrement the outer B.
  <1>5 [req] `lift.perm_call` is likewise one outer row.
  <1>6 [req] Executors MAY inline extensionally.
  <1>7 [req] Suite B runs one forward `lift.call` step and one forward `lift.ret` step of a recursive call, each followed by its inverse, and asserts that the complete pre-step tuple is restored and every frame field is clean.
ISA-3.7-P26
  <1>1 [req] Only oracles and lprocs declare locations, and every `regs` entry in them has type `reg[N]`; the `regs` list of an action, permutation action, CC or R function MUST be empty (an action's empty list may still carry `bounds`).   deps: ~ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)   declared: ISA-3.3-P1, ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-16.2
````

### §4.1 Classical control (CC, R)   [ISA-4.1]

````text
ISA-4.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] `const` | `r = const.iN c` |
ISA-4.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] `add sub mul and or xor shl lshr ashr` | `r = op a b` | wrap; shift ≥ N → 0
ISA-4.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] `udiv urem sdiv srem` | `r = op a b` | divisor 0 or signed MIN/−1: CC trap; R fault record (`FAULT_UB_DIV0` / `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW`, §4.9)   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap), ISA-4.9
ISA-4.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] `icmp` | `r = icmp.{eq,ne,ult,ule,slt,sle} a b` |
ISA-4.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] `select` | `r = select p a b` |
ISA-4.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] `zext sext trunc` | `r = op.iM a` |
ISA-4.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] `ptrtoint` / `inttoptr` | `r = ptrtoint.iM p` / `p = inttoptr a` | zero-extend/truncate to M / to ptr_width
ISA-4.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] `load` / `store` | `r = load.iN addr` / `store.iN addr v` | N/8 bytes little-endian, unaligned; outside every live allocation: CC trap / R fault record.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap)
  <1>2 [req] The suffix is always `.iN` with N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}: there is no pointer-typed load or store (a pointer is stored as `ptrtoint.iM` with M = ptr_width and reloaded with `inttoptr`)
ISA-4.1-P9
  <1>1 [req] `alloc` / `free` | `p = alloc n` / `free p` | §7.1   deps: ISA-7.1
ISA-4.1-P10
  <1>1 [req] `br` | `br ^b(args)` / `br p ^b1(args) ^b2(args)` | parallel block-parameter assignment
ISA-4.1-P11
  <1>1 [req] `call` / `ret` | `results = call @f args` / `ret vals` | arguments and values are bare operands (`r = call @f a b`, `(r1, r2) = call @f a`, `call @f`, `ret v`, `ret`); results bind in order; a parenthesised argument list is ill-formed for `call`; callee tiers per §3.1   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed), ISA-3.1
ISA-4.1-P12
  <1>1 [req] `uncall` | `uncall @f (args) (results)` | **segment uncall**, legal exactly under §7.4: the most recent completed call segment of the history is a call of `@f` whose recorded boundary argument and result values equal the current operand values, every frame after it is pure, and it contains, at any nesting depth, no `measure` frame and no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified; otherwise CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` with no state change beyond the fault record.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap), ISA-7.4
  <1>2 [req] The segment is reversed by the cancellation-aware history traversal of §7.4 (LIFO, nested segments included; frames already reversed and `uncall` markers skipped) and its frames are marked reversed; the `uncall` itself pushes a frame naming the segment   deps: ISA-5.5-P1.S5 (LIFO), ISA-7.4
ISA-4.1-P13
  <1>1 [req] `trap` | `trap` | CC trap; R fault record (`FAULT_UB_TRAP`, §4.9)   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap), ISA-4.9
ISA-4.1-P14
  <1>1 [req] `fault` | `fault C` | CC and R; literal 0 ≤ C < 2^64; one terminal record with code C for C ≠ 0, else 4 (§5.4); run ends `FAILED`, no successor executes, prefix history retained for reversal (§7.4, §10, §14.1).   deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.4, ISA-10, ISA-14.1
  <1>2 [req] One classical instruction (§8.2); in an oracle body or lproc, the one-budget-step §4.2 row instead   deps: ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-8.2, ISA-4.2
ISA-4.1-P15
  <1>1 [req] `qreg.new qreg.free qreg.slice qreg.concat qreg.width qreg.id qreg.from_id` | §3.3 | CC and R (§3.3)   deps: ISA-3.3
ISA-4.1-P16
  <1>1 [req] `measure` | `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | CC only; Born rule (§14.2); consumes q; `.iN` for a `qreg[*]` (§3.3)   deps: ISA-14.2, ISA-3.3
ISA-4.1-P17
  <1>1 [req] `budget.mint` | `tk = budget.mint @o s h st d` | CC and R; a token for the tuple (s, h, st, d) validated for oracle `@o` (§5.4)   deps: ISA-5.4
ISA-4.1-P18
  <1>1 [req] `effect` | `effect dev.op args` | R only (§7.3); results are written to memory operands   deps: ISA-7.3
ISA-4.1-P19
  <1>1 [req] `hostcall` | `rlen = hostcall service ptr len` | CC only (§10)   deps: ISA-10
ISA-4.1-P20
  <1>1 [req] **Measurement draws.** Supplied draws are consumed FIFO, one per executed measurement in dynamic execution order (including measurements of deterministic states); a failing precondition consumes no draw.
  <1>2 [req] Each draw is in [0,1).
  <1>3 [def] Enumerate outcomes lexicographically by their printed bits string, wire 0 first, and select the first outcome whose cumulative Born weight is **strictly greater** than the draw times the total weight.
  <1>4 [req] Thus an exact CDF tie selects the next nonzero-weight outcome.
  <1>5 [req] Returned integers remain little-endian (wire 0 is bit 0).
  <1>6 [req] This fixes the reference CLI convention; unsupplied draws follow the Born distribution (§14.2).   deps: ISA-14.2
ISA-4.1-P21
  <1>1 [req] **DRAW-ORDER witness**
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"draws":["0.25","0.75"],"integer_order_values":[1,3],"outcomes_bits":["01","11"…
ISA-4.1-P22
  <1>1 [unc] Prepare two fresh two-wire registers with H on both wires and measure them in order.
  <1>2 [req] Each has weights 1/4 on `00`, `01`, `10`, `11` in that order.
  <1>3 [req] The first draw selects `01` (integer 2) and the second `11` (integer 3).
  <1>4 [req] Reversed draw consumption would give integers 3,2; integer-ordered outcomes would give 1,3.
  <1>5 [req] Both draws test exact CDF ties.
````

### §4.2 Lifted rows (oracle bodies and lprocs)   [ISA-4.2]

````text
ISA-4.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] Columns: **Class**, **Reads**, **Writes / slots** (A: displaced values in slot order; D: the permutation), **Flag / fault** (C = code), **Succ**.
  <1>2 [req] Every row is one budget step; `k` is its own index.
ISA-4.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] `lift.const` | `w = lift.const.iN c` | A | – | w | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] `lift.binop` | `w = lift.{add,…,ashr} a b` | A | a, b | w | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] `lift.div` | `w = lift.{udiv,urem,sdiv,srem} a b` | A | a, b | w (under ¬flag) | flag = (b=0) ∨ (signed ∧ a=MIN ∧ b=−1); C = `FAULT_UB_DIV0` / `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT
ISA-4.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] `lift.icmp` | `w = lift.icmp.{pred} a b` | A | a, b | w:reg[1] | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] `lift.select` | `w = lift.select p a b` | A | p, a, b | w | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] `lift.cast` | `w = lift.{zext,sext,trunc}.iM a` | A | a | w | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P8
  <1>1 [req] `lift.mov` | `w = lift.mov a` | A | a | w | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P9
  <1>1 [req] `lift.parmov` | `(w_1..w_m) = lift.parmov (a_1..a_m)` | A | a_i | w_1..w_m (m slots) | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P10
  <1>1 [req] `lift.label` | `w = lift.label ^b` | A | – | w | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P11
  <1>1 [req] `lift.addr` | `w = lift.addr @g` | A | – | w = layout base of global or mapped region g | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P12
  <1>1 [req] `lift.boundscheck` | `lift.boundscheck p n` | D | p, alloctab | – | flag = ¬inrange(p, n); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT
ISA-4.2-P13
  <1>1 [req] `lift.load` | `w = lift.load.iN a` | A | the N/8 cells at a.. (§7.2) | w (under ¬flag) | flag = ¬inrange(a, N/8); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT   deps: ISA-7.2
ISA-4.2-P14
  <1>1 [req] `lift.store` | `lift.store.iN a v` | A | v | the N/8 cells at a.. (under ¬flag; one slot of N bits) | flag = ¬inrange(a, N/8); C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT
ISA-4.2-P15
  <1>1 [req] `lift.alloc` | `w = lift.alloc n` | A | n, heap_used, atop | under ¬flag, slots in this order: w ← heap_base + heap_used; alloctab[atop] ← (heap_base + heap_used, n, 1, 0); heap_used ← heap_used + max(n,1); atop ← atop + 1 (4 slots, all computed from the old state, then exchanged in this order, `alloctab[atop]` at the old `atop`).
  <1>2 [def] **Inverse exchanges**: atop, heap_used, alloctab[atop] (at the restored `atop`), w; then clear the four slots | flag = (heap_used + max(n,1) > heap_limit) ∨ (atop ≥ 2^A_tab); C = `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT
ISA-4.2-P16
  <1>1 [req] `lift.free` | `lift.free p` | D | p, alloctab | match_i = (i < atop) ∧ (base_i = p) ∧ ¬pinned_i into scratch (at most one i matches: unpinned entries are heap entries, whose bases strictly increase); under ¬flag: live_i ^= match_i for all i; scratch uncomputed from the unchanged atop, bases and pinned bits | flag = ¬∃ i: match_i ∧ live_i; C = `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT
ISA-4.2-P17
  <1>1 [req] `lift.faultif` | `lift.faultif p code` | D | p | – | flag = p; C = code, written as its effective cause C′ (§5.4) | ¬flag: k+1; flag: HALT   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S5 (effective cause), ISA-5.4
ISA-4.2-P18
  <1>1 [req] `lift.call` | `w = lift.call @f (a_1..a_m) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..)` (short form: §3.7) | A+D | §3.7 | §3.7 (slots: m capture slots `disp_1..disp_m`, then assignment slots for fp, sp, depth) | flag per §3.7; C = `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH` | ¬flag: entry of @f; flag: HALT   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S3 (capture slot), ISA-3.6-P2.S2 (assignment slot), ISA-3.7
ISA-4.2-P19
  <1>1 [req] `lift.ret` | `lift.ret v` | A+D | §3.7 | §3.7 (slots: the delivery slot `disp_1`, then assignment slots for fp, depth) | flag = (depth = 0); C = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` | ¬flag: `succ`; flag: HALT   deps: ISA-3.7-P18.S1 (`lift.ret v`), ISA-3.6-P2.S4 (delivery slot), ISA-3.6-P2.S2 (assignment slot), ISA-3.7
ISA-4.2-P20
  <1>1 [req] `lift.retval` | `lift.retval v` | D | v | result_val ^= v | – | HALT
ISA-4.2-P21
  <1>1 [req] `lift.oracle_call` | `lift.oracle_call @o tk (ins..) tgt fr (mem..) (args..)` | D | ins, tgt, fr, mem | tgt ^= payload; fr ^= code (callee contract) | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P22
  <1>1 [req] `lift.oracle_uncall` | same form | D | as above | the matched inverse (§5.5): the identical permutation | – | k+1   deps: ISA-5.5
ISA-4.2-P23
  <1>1 [req] `lift.perm_call` | `lift.perm_call @a (mut..) (ins..) (mem..) (args..)` | D | ins, mut, mem | mut ← P(mut, ins, mem); inverse = `adj` of @a | – | k+1
ISA-4.2-P24
  <1>1 [req] `qjump` | `qjump ^b` | D | – | – | – | ^b
ISA-4.2-P25
  <1>1 [req] `qbranch` | `qbranch p ^b1 ^b2` | D | p | – | – | p: ^b1; ¬p: ^b2
ISA-4.2-P26
  <1>1 [req] `qjumpr` | `qjumpr a` | D | a | succ ← a | flag = (a < first_f) ∨ (a ≥ first_f + n_f), where `[first_f, first_f + n_f)` are the row indices of the containing function instance (contiguous, §3.6); C = `FAULT_UB_TRAP` | ¬flag: `succ`; flag: HALT   deps: ISA-3.6
ISA-4.2-P27
  <1>1 [req] `fault` | `fault C` | D | – | code ^= C′; faulted ^= 1 (C′ the effective cause of C, §5.4) | – | HALT   deps: ISA-3.6-P2.S5 (effective cause), ISA-5.4
ISA-4.2-P28
  <1>1 [req] **`inrange(p, n)`** = ∃ i < atop: live_i ∧ base_i ≤ p ∧ p + n ≤ base_i + size_i, every comparison computed at width max(ptr_width, width(n)) + 1 (no wrap; §3.3); `lift.load`/`lift.store` carry this check **inherently** on their own address and length, so the reference function is total for every well-formed body; `lift.boundscheck` is an explicit assertion row the dialect emits before each access (after it the access's own flag is never set, so dialect-produced bodies' step counts are unchanged).   deps: ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [req] Rules: `lift.retval` is ill-formed in an lproc; `lift.ret` is ill-formed outside lprocs; `tk` is a token-typed classical argument of the calling oracle or a token parameter of the calling lproc.   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>3 [req] `lift.oracle_call`/`uncall`/`perm_call` carry the complete callee interface; `mem` operands name the calling oracle's mapped interface regions or the calling lproc's region parameters.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>4 [req] `lift.addr @g` names a global anywhere; it names an interface region **only in the body of the oracle that declares it** (lprocs are shared between oracles and receive region bases as parameters, as in §16.3 example 2).   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-16.3
  <1>5 [req] Label values obtained by `lift. label` denote first rows of blocks of the containing function instance; `qjumpr` to an index outside that instance's row range faults, so control never leaves a function except through `lift.call`/`lift.ret`; an in-range index that is not a block's first row enters that row.
  <1>6 [req] The `args` operands of `lift.oracle_call`, `lift.oracle_uncall` and `lift.perm_call` are classical arguments of the calling oracle or token and classical parameters of the calling lproc, in callee signature order and with exactly matching types, never locations.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>7 [req] They are fixed at entry (§3.1, §3.7).   deps: ISA-3.1, ISA-3.7
  <1>8 [req] Every lifted call available in an oracle body is also available in an lproc by passing the required token, classical arguments and mapped regions explicitly through `lift.call`.   deps: ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>9 [req] In lifted rows a width suffix `.iN`/`.iM` names a location width and has no upper bound (§3.3), except that `lift.load.iN`/`lift.store.iN` keep N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}.   deps: ISA-3.3
ISA-4.2-P29
  <1>1 [req] **Reference body and binding.** Every oracle carries its **reference body**: the stream the dialect's versioned mapping produced from the materialised source; hashed into `interface_hash` (§9.2).   deps: ISA-9.2
  <1>2 [req] F_B and the budget unit are defined solely by the reference interpreter (§14.1) on it; the object holds only the reference body; every executor optimisation is extensionally equal on D (T6).   deps: ISA-14.3-P6.S1 (T6), ISA-4.2-P29.S1 (reference body), ISA-14.1
````

### §4.3 Allocation   [ISA-4.3]

````text
ISA-4.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] `qalloc` | `w = qalloc cls n` | n clean wires of class `cls` or `any`; unconditional
ISA-4.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] `qborrow` | `w = qborrow cls n` | n wires in unknown state; usable only inside a borrow frame (§5.6)   deps: ISA-5.6
ISA-4.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] `qfree` | `qfree w` | release a `qalloc`ed bundle clean on every basis configuration   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
ISA-4.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] `qreturn` | `qreturn w` | matched inverse of `qborrow` after its frame
ISA-4.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] In addition to §3.3's operand rules, `qfree d` is well-formed iff every wire's current version is its `qalloc` version (including a version restored by a matched inverse) or the clean output of an explicit §6.4 cleanup of the value occupying that wire.   deps: ~ISA-3.3, ISA-6.4   declared: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.3-P1, ISA-5.1, ISA-6.4
````

### §4.4 Gates   [ISA-4.4]

````text
ISA-4.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] (code block, 6 lines) x = [[0,1],[1,0]] h = (1/√2)[[1,1],[1,-1]] s = diag(1,i) sdg = diag(1,-i)
ISA-4.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] Forms: `x a`, `cx c t`, `ccx c1 c2 t`, `mcx (c_1..c_k) t`, `swap a b`, `h a`, `s a`, `sdg a`, `t a`, `tdg a`, `cz a b`, `rx θ a`, `ry θ a`, `rz θ a`; all controlled on the active predicate.
  <1>2 [req] **Realisation**: permutation status is a property of the realised unitary at the gate boundary; conformant iff equal to the specified unitary exactly including global phase (ε = 0) or within declared ε in operator norm.
  <1>3 [req] **Oracles and permutation actions require ε = 0** for every gate; actions MAY use ε > 0 (§12).   deps: ISA-2-P3.S1 (permutation action), ISA-12
  <1>4 [req] A relative-phase Toffoli is never a conformant `ccx`.
````

### §4.5 Copy, XOR, exchange, in-place   [ISA-4.5]

````text
ISA-4.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] `qcopy` | `d = qcopy s` | d allocated clean; `cx s[i] d[i]` under the predicate
ISA-4.5-P2
  <1>1 [req] `quncopy` | `quncopy s d` | `cx s[i] d[i]` under the predicate; `qfree d`
ISA-4.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] `qxor` | `qxor s t` | `cx s[i] t[i]`
ISA-4.5-P4
  <1>1 [req] `qexch` | `qexch a b` | `swap a[i] b[i]`
ISA-4.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint.   deps: ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [req] **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11).   deps: ISA-11-P2.S1 (generated operations), ISA-11
  <1>3 [req] **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding.
  <1>4 [req] It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions).   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>5 [req] An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain.   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>6 [req] Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers.   deps: ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash), ISA-9.2-P3.S17 (closure hashes)
  <1>7 [req] Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap), ISA-4.2
  <1>8 [req] The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation.   deps: ISA-6, ISA-6.4
  <1>9 [req] Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled
````

### §4.6 Quantum memory, predication, structure   [ISA-4.6]

````text
ISA-4.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] `qregion` | `qregion name A W [@image]` | function-local region of 2^A cells of `wire[W]`, created at function entry (unconditional allocation, X gates from the data image, default zero), back at its image before exit, image gates reversed, cells released clean; scope is the function.
  <1>2 [req] `@image` is a `data` symbol reference (size 2^A·W/8 bytes, cell i = bits [iW, (i+1)W) of the little-endian image) and is part of the function's identity (§9.2)   deps: ISA-9.2
ISA-4.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] `qload` | `d = qload name a` | d clean; for each cell i: under `a == i`, `qxor cell_i d`
ISA-4.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] `qunload` | `qunload name a d` | inverse
ISA-4.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] `qstore` | `qstore name a v` | for each i: under `a == i`, `qxor v cell_i`
ISA-4.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] `qexchm` | `qexchm name a v` | for each i: under `a == i`, `qexch v cell_i`
ISA-4.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] `qpred`/`qunpred` | | §3.5   deps: ISA-3.5
ISA-4.6-P7
  <1>1 [req] `qif` | `qif w { then } { else }` | `qpred w; then; qunpred w; x w; qpred w; else; qunpred w; x w`
ISA-4.6-P8
  <1>1 [req] `qloop` | `qloop n { body }` | body n times
ISA-4.6-P9
  <1>1 [req] `qloop … while` | `qloop n while cond { body }` | `cond : wire[n]` permutation-provenance wires computed before the loop from wires the body does not modify; flags `c_1..c_n` clean; `cx cond[0] c_1`; iteration i under `qpred c_i`; after iteration i < n: `ccx c_i cond[i] c_{i+1}`; flags uncomputed in reverse and freed
ISA-4.6-P10
  <1>1 [req] A `qloop` body is threaded like a block: each visit resolves linear names against the bindings live at its entry, and its exit bindings supply the next visit's entry bindings; the entry and exit live names and their types MUST agree, with consumed body-local names absent from both.   declared: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.6-P8, ISA-4.6-P9
````

### §4.7 Region calls (structured code, CC and R)   [ISA-4.7]

````text
ISA-4.7-P1
  <1>1 [def] `oracle_call @o tk (ins..) tgt fr (mem..) (args..)`; `action_call @a (mut..) (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; `adj action_call …`; `adj oracle_call …` ≡ `oracle_call …`; `ctl w <call>` ≡ the call under `qpred w`.
  <1>2 [req] In CC and R, wire and region slots are filled by `qreg` operands per §3.3 (§3.1: R functions call Q regions exactly as CC does; the call is one frame of the R history, §7.4).   deps: ISA-3.3, ISA-3.1, ISA-7.4
  <1>3 [req] In an action, a region operand names an interface region of the caller or a local `qregion` of the caller (§4.6); a local region fills an `in` or a `mut` slot (an `in` slot returns it unchanged; the caller restores it to its image before exit either way, §5.1).   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region), ISA-4.6, ISA-5.1
  <1>4 [req] The `mem` operands of lifted rows follow §4.2.   deps: ISA-4.2
  <1>5 [req] **Classical arguments** have the signature's types `iN`, `ptr` (a value of width ptr_width), `f64` and `token`.
  <1>6 [req] In an oracle body or lproc an `iN` or `ptr` argument is a pseudo-location of width N or ptr_width, an `f64` argument a pseudo-location of width 64 holding its binary64 bits, and a `token` argument is usable only as the `tk` operand of `lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`, in a region call's `args` list, or in `lift.call`'s token list.   deps: ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>7 [req] Lproc classical parameters have these same bit readings and may also be forwarded in `lift.call`'s classical list; token parameters retain their token identity and validity (§5.4).   deps: ISA-5.4
````
