### §10 Launch ABI (launch 0.9)   [ISA-10]

````text
ISA-10-P1
  <1>1 [req] **Model.** A run executes a device CC entry; the host passes classical arguments, register ids and the entropy tape, receives classical results, fault codes and declared measurements; host services are reached by `hostcall`.
  <1>2 [def] **Positional binding**: argument i of `bennett_ run` binds to parameter i of the entry's signature: `INT(width, bytes)` to `iN` with width = N (else `E_ARG`), `F64` to `f64`, `QREG` to `qreg[n]` with the register's n or to `qreg[*]` with any width, `TOKEN` to `token` (a valid token, §5.4).   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>3 [req] Arguments and the entropy tape are **copied** at `bennett_run`; the caller may free its buffers afterwards.
  <1>4 [req] Host-minted tokens are scoped to the program and destroyed at unload; device-minted tokens to their run (§5.4).   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>5 [req] Accessor-returned pointers are valid until the owning object is freed.
  <1>6 [req] `bennett_unload` with a run not CLOSED: `E_INVALID_STATE`.
ISA-10-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Invocation tree.** A run record is a tree (CC segments, R calls, Q calls); an R call's subtree is owned by its R history; `bennett_reverse` reverses post-order: CC segments by their run history, R calls by LIFO reversal of their history (§7.4, reversed segments and `uncall` frames skipped), top-level Q calls by re-application / adjoint.   deps: ISA-5.5-P1.S5 (LIFO), ISA-7.4
  <1>2 [req] A fault record is terminal (§4.9): the run is `FAILED`, and every activation open at the fault keeps its frames, which reversal undoes like those of a call that returned.   deps: ISA-4.9
ISA-10-P3
  <1>1 [req] **States.** `CREATED → RUNNING → (SUSPENDED ⇄ RUNNING) → (HOST_REQUEST ⇄ RUNNING) → COMPLETED | FAILED`; `COMPLETED → REVERSED`; `FAILED → REVERSED`; any of `COMPLETED/FAILED/REVERSED → CLOSED` (`bennett_run_close` releases the record; register ids created by the run remain valid unless measured or freed).
  <1>2 [req] A run owns the registers and memory it touches exclusively while not CLOSED; a register reached by `qreg.from_id` is touched from then on; `bennett_qreg_prepare`, `bennett_qreg_free` and a `bennett_run` argument naming a register owned by another run that is not CLOSED are `E_INVALID_STATE`.
  <1>3 [req] **Reverse eligibility**: `COMPLETED` or `FAILED`; no `measure` frame; no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified (§17 item 14); no register it touched has been prepared, freed, sliced or used by another run since; else `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`.   deps: ISA-17-P15
  <1>4 [req] Reversing a `FAILED` run first undoes its terminal fault record, then traverses the rest of its history exactly as for a completed run, including the frames of activations that never returned; the run is then `REVERSED` with every component T4 names at its entry value.   deps: ISA-14.3-P4.S1 (T4), ISA-4.9-P1.S2 (terminal fault record)
  <1>5 [req] `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_ qreg_free` require a clean register (`E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` otherwise on a verifying executor; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14); there is no reset and no trace-out: a register in unknown state is released by a `measure` inside a run, which consumes its handle (§3.3).   deps: ISA-2-P9.S1 (non-verifying), ISA-17-P15, ISA-3.3
ISA-10-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Results.** `bennett_result_get` writes value i and sets `*kind` and `*width`: `INT` as ⌈N/8⌉ little-endian bytes, `*width` = N; `F64` as the 8 IEEE 754 binary64 little-endian bytes, `*width` = 64; `QREG` and `TOKEN` as the 8-byte little-endian id, `*width` = 64.
  <1>2 [req] A `cap` smaller than that byte count is `E_ARG` and nothing is written.
  <1>3 [req] Fault records are read by `bennett_result_nfaults` / `bennett_result_fault` (codes of §4.9; a `COMPLETED` run has none, a `FAILED` run has exactly one, its terminal record, and exposes no values).   deps: ISA-4.9
  <1>4 [req] For an R `fault C`, `bennett_wait` returns `OK` with `*result_out` set and `*request_out` NULL as for any `FAILED` run; `bennett_result_nvalues` is 0, `bennett_result_nfaults` is 1 and `bennett_result_fault(result, 0)` is exactly C when C ≠ 0 (4 when C = 0, §5.4), with all 64 bits preserved.   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>5 [req] The fault code is result data, not a `bennett_status` error or a host-service reply.
ISA-10-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Waiting.** `bennett_wait` blocks until the run leaves `RUNNING` and returns `OK` with exactly one outcome: `HOST_REQUEST` (`*request_out` set, `*result_out` NULL; `bennett_reply` answers it and the run is `RUNNING` again) or `COMPLETED` / `FAILED` (`*result_out` set, `*request_ out` NULL).
  <1>2 [req] Waiting on a `SUSPENDED`, `REVERSED` or `CLOSED` run is `E_ INVALID_STATE`.
ISA-10-P6
  <1>1 [req] **`hostcall`.** `rlen = hostcall service ptr len`: `[ptr, ptr + len)` MUST lie in one live allocation (else CC trap and no request is made); those bytes are copied into the request; the run enters `HOST_REQUEST`; the reply payload is written to `[ptr, ptr + min(len, reply length))` and `rlen` = the full reply length (so truncation is detectable; a reply whose length does not fit `ptr_width` bits is refused by `bennett_reply` with `E_ARG` before any write, and the run stays in `HOST_REQUEST`); the frame records the request bytes, the overwritten bytes and the reply bytes.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S1 (CC trap)
  <1>2 [req] Every `service` is declared in `devices.services`; an undeclared one is a load error.
ISA-10-P7
  <1>1 [req] **Tape.** `bennett_tape_next` yields each `effect` in tape order; `args` holds the operation's operands in the order of §7.3, each an unsigned 64-bit little-endian field (pointers zero-extended from `ptr_width`), so `*alen` = 8 × arity.   deps: ISA-7.3
  <1>2 [req] (code block, 53 lines) /* launch 0.9. All objects opaque. A function that returns an object transfers o…   deps: ISA-0.2-P12.S1 (K12)
````

### §10.1 Launch-estimate contract (K12)   [ISA-10.1]

````text
ISA-10.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] **LAUNCH-ESTIMATE** (K12; b-isa-spec-c15, from bennett-vm-hsd). The 0.9 request carries the complete resolved parameter vector.   deps: ISA-0.2-P12.S1 (K12)
ISA-10.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Resolved request.** `bennett_estimate_run` MUST copy and validate `params_jcs`, the JCS array of **all** classical arguments in signature order, encoded as §12.3 specifies; `params_len` is its UTF-8 byte length, excluding any NUL terminator.   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>2 [req] The separately supplied valid token of that program fixes `[B,heap,stack,depth]`; token-typed arguments fix their own tuples, never the outer tuple by default.
  <1>3 [req] Host- and live device-minted tokens are both admitted (§5.4); the request copies the tuple while valid, so later revocation cannot retarget an already returned estimate.   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>4 [req] An empty array binds an empty signature only; no argument is implicitly zero, inferred from a hash, or taken from an earlier run.
  <1>5 [req] A missing, extra, mistyped or out-of-range argument is `E_ARG` before producing an estimate.
  <1>6 [req] Every well-typed resolved argument vector is supported; the interface bundles, arbitrary target/fault entries, dirty wires and interface cells remain quantum inputs, not chosen basis values.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
ISA-10.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] The caller supplies a nonempty `variant` and `inherited_controls` as in §11; these are part of the request, not inferred from a different invocation.   deps: ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] The request denotes that region/variant/control count at its declared accuracy and those resolved parameters and budget.
  <1>3 [req] It uses the object's schedule exactly when every §5.4/§11 binding matches, otherwise the §11 defaults.   deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-11
  <1>4 [req] The estimator MUST resolve the complete generated realisation and aggregate its scheduled leaf events and wire live intervals by §8.2; running one basis input, counting only executed oracle rows instead of all padded B steps, or silently changing parameters, budget, target, expansion library or cleanup is not an estimate of that request.   deps: ISA-8.2-P2.S2 (leaf event), ISA-11-P2.S3 (expansion library), ISA-8.2
  <1>5 [req] A consumer lacking the selected expansion library reports `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires (b-isa-spec-8r4); this postpones mechanics, not the estimate capability.   deps: ISA-11
ISA-10.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Resource rows.** `estimate_nresources` counts scalar rows, not target resource declarations.
  <1>2 [req] A `count` or `time` resource has one row named by its escaped resource name.
  <1>3 [req] Each `occupancy` or `peak` resource has one row **for every target class**, including unused classes with value zero, named `escape(resource)[escape(class)]`; no sum across classes is substituted.
  <1>4 [req] `escape` percent-encodes the UTF-8 bytes except ASCII letters, digits, `-._~`, using uppercase hex (including `%`, `[` and `]`).
  <1>5 [req] Scalar resource names use the same escape.
  <1>6 [req] Rows are enumerated by increasing bytewise encoded name.
  <1>7 [req] Thus names are injective even when target names contain delimiters; `name` and `value_decimal` remain ordinary NUL-terminated strings with §10's ownership lifetime.   deps: ISA-10
ISA-10.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Decimal values.** Aggregation is exact rational arithmetic; rounding occurs only at the accessor boundary.
  <1>2 [req] `value_decimal` is the least nonnegative decimal with at most **34 significant digits** that is ≥ the exact resource value.
  <1>3 [req] For q > 0 let e be the unique integer with 10^e ≤ q < 10^(e+1); return `ceil(q / 10^(e−33)) × 10^(e−33)`.
  <1>4 [req] Zero is `0`.
  <1>5 [req] The canonical spelling uses ASCII digits and an optional decimal point, no exponent, plus sign, leading integer zeros (except `0`), trailing fractional zeros or negative zero.
  <1>6 [req] Exact integers and terminating rationals that fit are unchanged.
  <1>7 [req] This is an upper enclosure, not a fabricated exact value for 1/3; capacity, matching, objective comparison and certificate checking MUST use the exact rationals, never the rounded display.
ISA-10.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Certificate binding.** The estimate's certificate MUST bind object, interface, closure, target and effective schedule hashes, root variant/controls, accuracy, expansion identity and the resolved `bindings.invocation` (§12.3).   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>2 [req] Even a default-only realisation has an explicit empty-entries schedule document with those bindings, hence a schedule hash.
  <1>3 [req] Its root region has `estimate: { precision_digits: 34, exact_resources: { "<encoded row name>": { numerator: "<integer>", denominator: "<positive integer>" } } }`; fractions are reduced, nonnegative and decimal-string encoded, zero is 0/1.
  <1>4 [req] `resources` uses the identical encoded keys and canonical decimal strings returned by `estimate_get`.
  <1>5 [req] A checker MUST rebuild the bound realisation, recompute all exact rows and their upward decimal encodings, and reject any binding, key, rational or display mismatch.
  <1>6 [req] A producer's resource map alone is not verification.
ISA-10.1-P7
  <1>1 [unc] **LAUNCH-ESTIMATE witness**   deps: ISA-10.1-P1.S1 (LAUNCH-ESTIMATE)
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 30 lines) {
ISA-10.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] This **aggregation witness** assumes the displayed scheduled intervals, unit class rates and no other events; it does not assert an unbound oracle gate count.
  <1>2 [req] A permitted cost expression `1 / 3` produces that makespan.
  <1>3 [req] The exact class peaks are 2 and 1, not a replacement scalar 3; occupancies are 2/3 and 1/3, and the idle class is present.
  <1>4 [req] Changing the argument `2` to `1` denotes a different request, even at the same budget.
  <1>5 [req] No 0.8 request can transmit that distinction through §10's signature.   deps: ISA-10
````

### §11 Scheduling interface (sched 0.9)   [ISA-11]

````text
ISA-11-P1
  <1>1 [req] (code block, 19 lines) typedef struct bennett_sched_request_t bennett_sched_request_t; typedef struct b…
ISA-11-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Generated operations and the expansion library.** Every row and instruction expands into **generated operations** (gates, allocations, releases).   deps: ISA-11-P2.S3 (expansion library)
  <1>2 [req] Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6 and §5.2 (dispatch comparators k = 0..n in index order; a row's sub-operations in listed order; successor XORs; commit; cleanup).   deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-4.2, ISA-4.5, ISA-4.6, ISA-5.2
  <1>3 [req] Below that level (the gate decomposition of comparators, adders, multiplexers, `inrange` scans and their scratch) the expansion is defined by an **expansion library**: a versioned document, identified in the schedule's `bindings.expansion` as `{ "name", "version", "hash" }`, that lists for every row kind and every instruction its generated operations in order, with their **ordinals**, scratch identities and the subset it marks removable, and that is extensionally equal to the reference (§5.7, T6).   deps: ISA-14.3-P6.S1 (T6), ISA-11-P2.S1 (generated operations), ISA-5.7
  <1>4 [req] A consumer that does not implement the bound library rejects the schedule (`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`); two consumers implementing it execute and charge the same operation set.
  <1>5 [req] `bennett-isa-spec` publishes the reference library `ref` (§17 item 7).   deps: ISA-17-P8
  <1>6 [req] §5.7 transformations under a schedule MUST keep the ordinals of the operations they keep and MAY remove only operations the library marks removable.   deps: ISA-5.7
ISA-11-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Schedule document** (JCS) has the following schema, including reserved support points and optional instance/call-occurrence selectors.   declared: ISA-11-P2, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P22, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>2 [req] (code block, 13 lines) { "bindings": { "closure_hash", "target_hash", "region", "variant", "inherited_c…
ISA-11-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Topology placement.** With a §8.3 topology, `interface_vertices` lists inherited control wires first as individual width-one arrays, then one vertex array per source interface slot in §9.4 slot order; array elements follow bit order (and region cell address then bit order).   deps: ISA-8.3, ISA-9.4
  <1>2 [req] A callee inherits its actual operand placements.
  <1>3 [req] `alloc.vertices`, when present, assigns every bit of that allocation in bit order.
  <1>4 [req] These arrays MUST have the declared width, resolve to the correct classes and have disjoint live uses; without a topology they MUST be absent.
  <1>5 [req] Omitted placements use the lowest-index free vertex of the resolved class, assigning bits in order; interface slots are assigned first in slot order, then allocations in generated order.
  <1>6 [req] Vertices are retained for a wire's lifetime and freed on release; borrowed wires retain their owner's vertices.
  <1>7 [req] Every native leaf, including scheme leaves, MUST satisfy §8.3 adjacency, else `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming §17 item 15.   deps: ISA-8.3, ISA-17-P16
  <1>8 [req] No routing is inserted.
ISA-11-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Matching.** Every generated operation has an **invocation chain**: the program points of the region calls and `lift.oracle_call`s that led to it, outermost first, ending in its own point.   declared: ISA-4.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-3.6
  <1>2 [req] `lift.call` adds no element: an lproc row in an oracle's closure generates its expansion once per fixed row instance per step under its dispatch predicate, independently of which `lift.call` reached that instance or the recursion depth.   deps: ~ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)   declared: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6
  <1>3 [req] `path` matches an operation iff it is a **prefix** of that chain, element by element: `symbol` and `point` equal, and `iteration` either `*` or equal to the vector of 0-based dynamic repetition counts of the `qloop`s enclosing that point (outermost first; its length is the point's loop nesting depth, `[]` outside every loop); `instance` and `call_index` match as specified below.   declared: ISA-4.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>4 [req] An oracle invocation's forward step coordinate is t ∈ 1..B and its inverse step t has `phase: rev`; the root B is `bindings.budget[0]` and a nested oracle's B is the steps component of its own call token, not the parent's B.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4, ISA-10
  <1>5 [req] An entry's `step` and `phase` read the oracle invocation entered by the last oracle-call element of its `path`, or the root invocation if there is no such element.   declared: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2-P3
  <1>6 [req] A call element enters that invocation only when it is followed along the operation's chain into the callee: when the element is the operation's own final point, it remains in its containing invocation.   declared: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7
  <1>7 [req] `lift.call` never changes this level; a path ending at a call element that is a proper prefix of the operation's chain already selects the entered callee level.   declared: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7, ISA-4.7
  <1>8 [req] At a level with no oracle step, including an action root and oracle construction/commit/release outside the steps, only `step: "*"` and `phase: "*"` match; an action's adjoint does not itself supply a `rev` phase.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.3, ISA-4.7
  <1>9 [req] `generated` is the ordinal within the row's expansion in the bound library, or within its reserved support pseudo-point (K64), or the non-emitting cleanup carrier ordinal (K65).   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P64.S1 (K64), ~ISA-0.2-P65.S1 (K65)   declared: ISA-11-P2.S3, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P29, ISA-6.4
ISA-11-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Specificity** is the lexicographic tuple (path length, number of non-`*` iterations, step non-`*`, phase non-`*`, generated non-`*`); an entry applies iff all its selectors match, and among matching entries of the same kind the unique maximal one applies.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-4.6
  <1>2 [req] Two distinct entries (distinct array positions, even if identical) with equal maximal specificity for the same generated operation and kind make a tie; this is the meaning of "incomparable", and document order is never significant.   declared: ISA-11-P6.S1
  <1>3 [req] A tie makes the schedule invalid for that operation under the validation stages below; overlapping equal-specificity entries do not make a tie where a more-specific matching entry applies, including coverage by a union of such entries.   declared: ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P7
  <1>4 [req] Operations outside an invocation's own budget or inside a zero-trip loop are not generated and cannot witness a tie.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-4.6
  <1>5 [req] Selectors are resolved independently per `kind`.
  <1>6 [req] A `gate` entry matches only gate operations, an `alloc` entry only allocations, and a `start` entry every generated operation.   declared: ISA-11-P2.S1, ISA-11-P3
  <1>7 [req] The applying entry supplies the decision: an omitted field with a default takes that default, never a value from a less-specific matching entry.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17
  <1>8 [req] Defaults for uncovered operations: `reverse`; `native` if the gate matches a `gates` entry, else the first matching scheme; class = the first class declared in the target's `classes` object (JCS order) that has capacity for it; sequential timing in generated order.
  <1>9 [req] An operation uncovered for one kind, including in a nested invocation, takes that kind's default directly, without inserting an entry or altering any other operation's selected decision.   declared: ISA-8.3, ISA-17
  <1>10 [req] There is no requirement that these defaults be expressible by additional entries in the single-`step`, single-`phase` selector language.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17
  <1>11 [req] **Timing**: a `start` time MUST be ≥ the end of every operation the operation depends on (data, wire lifetime, region cell version, effect order); an entry violating this makes the schedule invalid.
  <1>12 [req] **Obligations**: satisfy §6; respect capacity; report every resource; be reproducible from the request plus scheduler version.   deps: ISA-6
ISA-11-P7
  <1>1 [req] **Validation stages (K55).** A consumer validates every binding and every path against the object, including the maximal-match condition at the stages specified here, and executes verbatim or rejects with `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P55.S1 (K55)   declared: ISA-9.2, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3, ISA-11-P6.S4, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3, ISA-11-P7.S7
  <1>2 [def] For admission, a nested invocation budget is **object-derivable** exactly when substituting literal integer constants and signature-positional classical/token forwarding into the four operands of `budget.mint` yields one tuple for that call site; an unresolved root parameter, conflicting tuples or any other unresolved operand makes it non-derivable.   declared: ISA-4.1, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10
  <1>3 [req] Literal propagation initialises every nonliteral value to unknown, takes exact bit-vector values from `const.iN`, copies values through positional call/return and branch/block-parameter bindings to a fixed point, and joins all syntactic incoming values as unknown unless they are all the same known value; arithmetic, predicate reasoning and additional implementation-specific inference do not make a budget object-derivable for this staging rule.   deps: ~ISA-11-P7.S2 (object-derivable)   declared: ISA-4.1, ISA-3.4, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.4
  <1>4 [req] The root region's token parameters remain unresolved at object admission even if a CC/R caller in the object mints a literal-budget token, since that caller does not bind every invocation of the region.   declared: ISA-4.1, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10
  <1>5 [req] This derivation does not invert `params_hash`, assume a token id's tuple, or substitute an enclosing invocation's budget.   declared: ISA-9.2, ISA-12.3, ISA-5.4
  <1>6 [req] A consumer MUST reject root-level ties and nested ties whose invocation domains are object-derivable at admission, and MUST reject a tie in a non-derivable nested domain at the first concrete operation in generated order having multiple maximal matching entries, before applying any realisation to that operation, with `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` and the diagnostic "§11: multiple maximal matching schedule entries".   deps: ISA-11-P7.S2 (object-derivable), ~ISA-11   declared: ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3, ISA-11-P7.S7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10
  <1>7 [req] A nested domain is object-derivable only if every oracle budget on its invocation chain is object-derivable (the root uses the bound budget); admission quantifies over the generated operations of these domains, and defers the remaining domains without rejecting merely because some possible budget would contain a tie.   deps: ISA-11-P7.S2 (object-derivable), ISA-11-P5.S1 (invocation chain), ~ISA-11-P2.S1 (generated operations)   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-4.6, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3
  <1>8 [req] Admission success therefore leaves the prescribed concrete checks in non-derivable domains outstanding, even when actual token values become available earlier; learning them does not move rejection to a different stage.   declared: ISA-5.4, ISA-10, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S6, ISA-11-P7.S7
  <1>9 [req] Schedule decimal numbers and decimal strings denote the exact rational of their decimal text; equal values match and resolve identically regardless of spelling.   declared: ISA-8.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P1
  <1>10 [req] Token parameters in `params_hash` use §12.3's canonical four-component tuple encoding, for host- and device-minted tokens alike.   deps: ISA-12.3   declared: ISA-12.3, ISA-5.4
ISA-11-P8
  <1>1 [def] **Selection witnesses** *(non-normative)*.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-5.2-P3
  <1>2 [req] For root B=2 and nested B=3, nested step 3 exists; with nested B=2 it does not, independently of root B.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4
  <1>3 [req] A path of `[]` with `step: 1` reads root step 1 even for an operation at nested step 3; a path through the nested call reads nested step 3.   declared: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2-P3
  <1>4 [req] Two `lift.call` sites p1/p2 into the same lproc row q give one row-q expansion per fixed instance per enclosing step, with neither p1 nor p2 in its chain; different binding keys yield distinct instance components.   deps: ~ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)   declared: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6
  <1>5 [req] An action-root X has no step/phase, including under adjoint.   declared: ISA-5.3, ISA-4.7
  <1>6 [req] Equal maximal entries A/B reject; A/B fully covered by higher C or a union C1/C2 admit in every order; duplicate C entries reject.   deps: ~ISA-3.7-P12.S1 (C1), ~ISA-3.7-P13.S1 (C2)   declared: ISA-11-P3
  <1>7 [req] An alloc entry cannot shadow a gate entry.   declared: ISA-11-P2.S1, ISA-11-P3
  <1>8 [req] A more-specific realisation-only entry takes the default share even if a broad entry writes a share.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17
  <1>9 [req] For root-step-1 scheme X and an uncovered root-step-2/nested-step-1 gate, only the latter takes its default; no synthetic nested entry overrides the former.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17
  <1>10 [req] An unknown nested budget permits admission, but if the first ambiguous operation is at nested step 3, B=3 rejects there before its realisation, whereas B=2 has no such operation; an object-derivable B=3 rejects at admission.   deps: ~ISA-11-P7.S2 (object-derivable)   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4, ISA-10
  <1>11 [req] Numeral 0 and strings `"0"`, `"0.0"`, `"0.000"` all denote zero.   declared: ISA-8.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P1
ISA-11-P9
  <1>1 [unc] **Invocation-edge identity (K63).** Every invocation edge identifies the source call point, the containing fixed-binding instance (the §3.7 integer binding key, or `[]` outside an lproc instance), and the zero-based semantic-call occurrence within that source row expansion, serialized as `instance` and `call_index` alongside `symbol`, `point`, `iteration`.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P63.S1 (K63), ~ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ~ISA-3.7   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>2 [req] Number semantic calls in forward instantiated constructor traversal order before descending into callees, counting `call` constructors rather than `invoke`/`apply` recipe composition, independently of leaf ordinals; inverse traversal retains forward occurrence numbers.   declared: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P29.S3
  <1>3 [req] The operation's terminal point also carries its containing `instance`, but has no `call_index` unless it is an invocation edge into a callee.   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>4 [req] In schedule path elements each new selector may be `*`, and omission means `*`; a concrete `instance` matches that exact integer array, and a concrete `call_index` matches only an edge with that occurrence number.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>5 [req] Validate concrete instance keys against §3.7 source instance formation and concrete call indices against the bound-library expansion at that source point and instance, including static repetitions.   deps: ~ISA-3.7   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>6 [req] These selectors do not add specificity components: the existing lexicographic tuple is unchanged, so equally specific overlapping entries still tie unless covered by a more-specific match.   declared: ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3
  <1>7 [req] The edge's source point selects the entered invocation for K52 exactly as before; adding instance and call occurrence does not introduce a `lift.call` invocation or another step level.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P52.S1 (K52)   declared: ISA-11-P5.S5, ISA-11-P5.S6, ISA-11-P5.S7, ISA-3.7-P6
ISA-11-P10
  <1>1 [req] **Non-row addresses (K64).** Non-row support leaves have reserved pseudo-points `$construct`, `$step`, `$commit`, `$release` in their containing oracle; these are legal path points rather than user block names.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P64.S1 (K64)   declared: ISA-3.6, ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P22
  <1>2 [req] For the bound `oracle.construct` recipe, its initial `visit State` subtree has point `$construct`, each forward or inverse `step` invocation has point `$step`, the commit segment from allocation of `halt` through release of `halt` has point `$commit`, and the final inverse `visit State` subtree has point `$release`.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11.1-P22.S14, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>3 [req] Within `step`, dispatch comparisons, HALT successor support, pc/next/frame exchanges and dispatch cleanup stay at `$step`; `Rows[k]` and `Next[k]` source-template subtrees instead retain source row k's point and shared row ordinal space in traversal order, and semantic callees retain their own invocation chains and spaces.   deps: ~ISA-11-P5.S1 (invocation chain)   declared: ISA-3.6, ISA-11.1-P14, ISA-11.1-P22.S14, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>4 [req] All nested helper `invoke`, loops and descriptor visits inherit their containing point unless they enter one of those source-row or semantic callee subtrees.   declared: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P18, ISA-11.1-P22
  <1>5 [req] Number support leaves from zero in instantiated expansion order within each pseudo-point, separately for each oracle step and phase at `$step`, excluding all source-row and callee subtrees; inverse traversal retains the corresponding forward step's ordinals and occurrence numbers.   declared: ISA-11.1-P29.S1, ISA-11.1-P29.S2, ISA-11.1-P29.S3, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>6 [req] Reversing a complete already expanded construction retains each leaf's forward pseudo-point and ordinal while adjointing its operation.   declared: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P29.S3
  <1>7 [req] `$step` carries the containing oracle's t and fwd/rev phase; the other three points have no step or phase at that level and match only wildcard step/phase there, including when B=0.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P5.S8
  <1>8 [req] A pseudo-point's own instance and source-qloop iteration vectors are `[]`; outer call edges retain their actual coordinates, and bound-library constructor loops do not create source-qloop iterations.   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-4.6, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P5
  <1>9 [req] Path validation accepts exactly these reserved spellings for oracle support and validates their generated domains by the partition above; B=0 has no `$step` leaves but still has construction, commit and release.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P7, ISA-11.1-P22
  <1>10 [req] These addresses annotate the bound event stream without removing, reordering or changing the charge of any emitted leaf.   declared: ISA-11-P2, ISA-8.2, ISA-11.1-P29
ISA-11-P11
  <1>1 [req] **Whole-cleanup selection (K65).** Each explicit computing-sequence/cleanup pair has one non-emitting cleanup carrier at its source cleanup point, identified before realisation expansion, even when the cleanup emits no leaves.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P65.S1 (K65)   declared: ISA-6.2-P1, ISA-6.4-P1, ISA-11-P3
  <1>2 [req] The point is the explicit `quncopy`, `qunload`, `lift.oracle_uncall` or adjoint-call point when that instruction is the cleanup; for an explicitly written inverse sequence ending in `qfree` or `qreturn`, it is that terminating release point (also for an empty inverse sequence).   declared: ISA-4.3, ISA-4.5, ISA-4.6, ISA-4.2, ISA-5.3, ISA-5.5, ISA-6.2-P1
  <1>3 [req] The carrier uses that point's containing invocation chain, fixed-binding instance, source-qloop iteration vector and containing step/phase, with carrier ordinal 0; it creates no callee edge or leaf ordinal.   deps: ISA-11-P5.S1 (invocation chain)   declared: ISA-11-P5, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-4.6, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>4 [req] Only cleanup-kind entries match the carrier, with `generated: "*"` or `generated: 0`; other generated integers do not match, and gate, alloc and start entries do not match it.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-11-P6.S5, ISA-11-P6.S6
  <1>5 [req] Resolve the unique maximal cleanup entry or the reverse default before expanding that cleanup, and apply its reverse/recompute realisation to the entire paired cleanup, subject to §6's validity conditions.   deps: ~ISA-6   declared: ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S7, ISA-11-P6.S8, ISA-6.2, ISA-6.4
  <1>6 [req] The carrier emits no gate, allocation, release, start event, resource charge or timing dependency; ordinary gate/alloc/start selection then applies to the chosen realisation's emitted leaves, and cleanup-kind entries do not match those leaves.   declared: ISA-8.2, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11-P6.S6, ISA-6.4
  <1>7 [req] Internal constructor uncomputation, implicit qregion image restoration, loop-flag cleanup and the certified private qinplace t/f cleanup keep their instruction-defined expansions and do not add explicit-pair carriers.   declared: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-4.6, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-6.4
  <1>8 [req] For `d = qcopy s; quncopy s d`, selecting recompute at the quncopy point chooses fresh-copy/XOR/uncompute for the whole cleanup, while reverse chooses inverse-copy/release at the same carrier; an empty inverse sequence still has its ordinal-0 carrier at its release point.   declared: ISA-4.5, ISA-6.2-P1, ISA-6.4-P1, ISA-11-P6
````

### §11.1 Reference-library contract (K11)   [ISA-11.1]

````text
ISA-11.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] **ISA-REF-LIB** (K11; b-isa-spec-8r4, from bennett-vm-47t). This is the decided 0.9 contract.   deps: ISA-0.2-P11.S1 (K11)
  <1>2 [req] The complete serialized constructor catalog `spec/ref-0.9-draft.json` is produced by a separate job under b-isa-spec-8r4; `ref/` is published after 0.9 acceptance.
  <1>3 [req] Until the bound library is available, consumers report `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires; staged conformance cases remain unavailable naming b-isa-spec-8r4.   deps: ISA-11
  <1>4 [req] The witness below is not a full-library hash.
ISA-11.1-P2
  <1>1 [unc] **Serialization and identity.** Publish `ref/README.md` and `ref/ref-0.9.json` after acceptance.
  <1>2 [req] The JSON file is RFC 8785 canonical UTF-8, with no trailing LF, containing exactly `isa`, `name`, `version` and `recipes`.
  <1>3 [req] The first three are strings `0.9`, `ref`, `0.9`.
  <1>4 [req] `recipes` is an array in bytewise `kind` order, then increasing parameter tuple order.
  <1>5 [req] Each instantiated recipe has `kind`, its integer width or other named integer parameters, `operations`, `scratch`, `removable` and `source` (the motivating ISA sentence).
  <1>6 [req] A parameterised recipe uses `parameters` (an ordered array of formals defined below) and `body` instead of the instance's `operations`; its body uses the closed constructors below.
  <1>7 [req] Recipes cover every §4 instruction/row, the §3.6 step, §3.7 activation operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.   deps: ISA-4, ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.2
  <1>8 [req] Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2), not invented quantum gates.   deps: ISA-8.2-P2.S2 (leaf event), ISA-8.2
  <1>9 [req] The README documents schema, parameters, coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.
ISA-11.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF, including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded hash field:
  <1>2 [req] (code block, 6 lines) isa=0.9
ISA-11.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] `bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex hash.
  <1>2 [req] This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines; it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.   deps: ISA-9.2
  <1>3 [req] Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's `bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic envelope ... every part ends with LF".
  <1>4 [req] A changed recipe, source, scratch name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).   deps: ISA-12.1
ISA-11.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an allocation (`alloc`, `width`, `class`, `state`), release (`release`), classical event (`classical`, `point`), counted loop (`for`, `from`, `to`, `body`), named recipe expansion (`invoke`, ordered `args`), or inverse (`inverse`, `body`).
  <1>2 [req] Constructor objects have exactly those keys.
  <1>3 [req] Fully instantiated `operations` contain only emitted leaves, each also carrying its computed `ordinal`; unexpanded `body` constructors carry no ordinal.
  <1>4 [req] Scalars are integer literals, parameter names, or prefix arrays `[op, operands…]` for `+ - * div mod bit`; div is floor division with a positive divisor.
  <1>5 [req] `bit(x,i)` requires nonnegative integers x and i and returns floor(x/2^i) mod 2, with no fixed machine-word truncation (K23).   deps: ISA-0.2-P23.S1 (K23)
  <1>6 [req] For x=13, bit(x,0..4) is 1,0,1,1,0; literal construction emits X only for the one bits, in increasing bit order.
  <1>7 [req] Wires are declaration names with integer-expression subscripts.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (Wire)
  <1>8 [req] Loops enumerate [from,to) in increasing order; expansion is depth first, array order, and recipe dependencies are acyclic.
  <1>9 [req] An inverse reverses its fully expanded body and adjoints each leaf by §5.3, interchanging allocation/release.   deps: ISA-5.3
  <1>10 [req] The complete serialized catalog must encode the algorithms below in these constructors before release; this draft does not claim that prose alone is an interchangeable file.
ISA-11.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Typed binding (K19).** A formal is `{ "name": name, "type": type }`, with exactly these keys, except `wire`, which additionally requires `width`, and `templates`, which additionally requires `length` (integer expressions using earlier integer formals); `arguments` requires `symbol`, and `value` requires `isa_type`, as specified in K24; K25–K27 add `records`, `region`, `class`, and `allocation` with the keys specified there.   deps: ISA-0.2-P19.S1 (K19), ISA-0.2-P24.S1 (K24), ISA-0.2-P25.S1 (K25), ISA-0.2-P26.S1 (K26), ISA-0.2-P27.S1 (K27)
  <1>2 [req] Types are `integer`, `wire`, `angle`, `body`, `symbol`, `environment`, `event`, `image`, `templates`, `arguments`, `value`, `records`, `region`, `class`, and `allocation`.
  <1>3 [req] A bare name abbreviates an `integer` formal, retaining the printed witness bytes.
  <1>4 [req] Names are unique within a parameter list.
  <1>5 [req] Width expressions use only earlier integer formals.
  <1>6 [req] A wire formal of width n is the ordered list w[n], with little-endian indexing, including the empty list when n = 0.
ISA-11.1-P7
  <1>1 [unc] `invoke` binds `args` positionally, evaluating actuals in the caller's lexical environment before binding any callee formal.
  <1>2 [req] Integer actuals use the scalar grammar above.
  <1>3 [req] A wire actual is a wire name, a subscripted wire, an array of wire actuals concatenated left to right, or exactly `{ "range": name, "from": scalar, "to": scalar }`, selecting [from,to) from a named bundle; bounds must satisfy 0 ≤ from ≤ to ≤ its width.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>4 [req] An actual's resulting length must equal its formal's evaluated width.
  <1>5 [req] In a gate's `wires` array a whole formal emits all its wires in order; this encodes `mcx(k)` as k controls followed by one target, including k=0.
  <1>6 [req] `{ "each": name, "in": actual, "body": [...] }` additionally visits wire-list elements in order, binding name as a one-wire formal for each visit.
  <1>7 [req] Its binding shadows lexically and is restored after the loop.
ISA-11.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] A body actual is an ordered constructor array, captured with its caller's lexical environment, or the name of an existing body formal.
  <1>2 [req] Exactly `{ "splice": name }` expands it at that point.
  <1>3 [req] Substitution is capture-free: caller references never resolve to callee locals, loop and `each` bindings have lexical scope, and every fresh allocation identity is derived from its expansion occurrence path (including loop iteration, body-splice occurrence and invocation path).
  <1>4 [req] Allocations must not shadow a live declaration in the same scope; a release names its matching live allocation.
  <1>5 [req] An inverse traverses constructors in reverse, toggles the adjoint on nested bodies and uses the same restored bindings at each position; allocation declarations supply release/adjoint metadata even when allocation and release occur in different nested constructor lists.
ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>1 [req] A symbol actual is a 64-lowercase-hex §9.2 closure hash, or a bound symbol formal.   deps: ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash), ISA-9.2
  <1>2 [req] The linked closure registry supplies that symbol's own typed interface and constructor body; the hash must match the linked closure, not an arbitrary supplied body.
  <1>3 [req] Exactly `{ "call": name, "args": [...] }` expands that symbol under a new invocation-path element identified by the call constructor's source point, containing fixed-binding instance and forward semantic-call occurrence number as serialized in §11.   deps: ISA-11   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>4 [req] It retains its own ordinal space starting at zero.   declared: ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P29.S2
  <1>5 [req] `invoke` is recipe composition within a row; `call` is a semantic symbol invocation and never flattens callee ordinals into the caller's space.   declared: ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P29.S2
  <1>6 [req] Repeated calls have distinct paths through their `call_index`, even when callee hash and arguments agree.   declared: ISA-11, ISA-11.1-P29.S2
ISA-11.1-P10
  <1>1 [req] The inherited effective-control set is always passed to callees and body splices.
  <1>2 [req] An `environment` actual uses the wire-actual grammar and adds its wires to that set (set union, repeated controls counted once).
  <1>3 [req] Exactly `{ "under": actual, "body": [...] }` adds controls for its lexical body and restores the outer set afterwards.
  <1>4 [req] Controls apply to semantic gates only, never allocation/release or classical events (§3.5/§4.3).   deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.3
  <1>5 [req] A control may coincide with a gate's explicit control, but never with a target (including a diagonal-gate target), as §3.3 requires.   deps: ISA-3.3
  <1>6 [req] Gate operands must otherwise be distinct.
ISA-11.1-P11
  <1>1 [req] An `event` actual is exactly `{ "instruction": text, "point": text }`, or an event formal name.
  <1>2 [req] `instruction` is one complete canonical §9.1 classical instruction with its operand bindings; `point` identifies its §8.2 classical execution point.   deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-8.2
  <1>3 [req] `{ "classical": opcode, "point": event_name }` emits that bound event (its opcode must match, allowing a concrete `.iN` width suffix) without executing or substituting its instruction text; classical semantics and retained history are §4.1/§7.4.   deps: ISA-4.1, ISA-7.4
  <1>4 [req] Literal classical leaves retain their existing `classical`/`point` form.
  <1>5 [req] A classical event is not a unitary; an `inverse` containing one requires retained §7.4 history and is not a quantum-library matrix witness.   deps: ISA-7.4
ISA-11.1-P12
  <1>1 [req] Consumers MUST reject the library document on arity/type/width mismatch, unbound name or closure hash, invalid wire range, duplicate formal, nonfinite angle, unknown constructor/key/gate, invalid gate arity, coincident target/control, unmatched allocation/release, or cyclic `invoke`/symbol expansion.   deps: ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash)
  <1>2 [req] Static integer bounds must yield finite expansion; validation is repeated for every actual parameter tuple before its expansion can be used.
  <1>3 [req] There are no implicit bindings or defaults.
  <1>4 [req] Acyclicity concerns declared recipe/symbol dependencies, not repetition of a higher-order recipe name inside finite captured actual syntax: qloop(3, qloop(2, B)) expands to six copies of B.
  <1>5 [req] Consumers expand finite actual bodies to a constructor fixpoint.
ISA-11.1-P13
  <1>1 [req] **Image binding (K21).** An `image` actual is a bound image formal or an even-length string of lowercase hexadecimal byte pairs in data-image byte order.   deps: ISA-0.2-P21.S1 (K21)
  <1>2 [req] The empty string denotes the absent/default-zero image, including on a wire list whose width is not byte-aligned.
  <1>3 [req] Exactly `{ "image": name, "wires": actual }` emits no leaf for the empty image; a nonempty image requires exactly eight wires per byte; bit 8j+i of the flattened wire list is bit i of byte j (i=0..7).
  <1>4 [req] It emits one X leaf for each one bit in increasing bit order, with an empty effective-control set regardless of inherited controls.
  <1>5 [req] This constructor denotes only the unconditional data-image preparation or restoration required by §3.5/§4.6; ordinary X gates remain semantic.   deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6
  <1>6 [req] The bytes are serialized in the actual, not resolved from an unbound external image name.
  <1>7 [req] Image leaves have ordinary row ordinals and the same allocation wire identities as semantic leaves.
  <1>8 [req] Inverse traverses these X leaves in reverse order, still unconditional.
  <1>9 [req] Reject malformed hex, unbound image or a byte/wire width mismatch.
  <1>10 [req] Witness: `"8102"` on 16 wires emits X at indices 0,7,9; inverse emits 9,7,0, even under a false inherited predicate.
  <1>11 [req] This fixes the image binding bytes without altering §9.2's source data-symbol identity.   deps: ISA-9.2
ISA-11.1-P14
  <1>1 [req] **Indexed typed body templates (K22).** A `templates` actual is an ordered array of exactly `length` objects, each with exactly `parameters` and `body`, or the name of a bound templates formal.   deps: ISA-0.2-P22.S1 (K22)
  <1>2 [req] Each object's parameters use the same typed formal grammar as recipes; its body uses the same closed constructors.
  <1>3 [req] The array is captured in the caller's lexical environment.
  <1>4 [req] Exactly `{ "apply": name, "index": scalar, "args": [...] }` selects its zero-based element and binds positional actuals evaluated at the apply site, with the same type/width/arity validation as invoke.
  <1>5 [req] Formal bindings shadow captured names only inside that application; all free references still resolve in the captured environment.
  <1>6 [req] Effective controls are the union of captured controls, apply-site controls and explicit environment actuals.
  <1>7 [req] Fresh allocations include the application occurrence in their identity.
  <1>8 [req] Applications compose within the containing row's ordinal space; they do not introduce a semantic symbol call.
  <1>9 [req] Reject negative/out-of-range indices, length mismatch, unknown/duplicate formals or invalid constructor syntax even in unused templates.
  <1>10 [req] Inverse reverses the applications and adjoints each selected body using its restored positional bindings.
  <1>11 [req] This permits row templates with a typed frame formal, applied to frame_t, and a step template applied to each successive frame without rebinding a captured free name.
  <1>12 [req] Witness: two templates `(f:wire[2])` containing CX outer f[0] and CX outer f[1], applied in index order to a, emit CX outer a[0], CX outer a[1]; the inverse emits them in reverse order.
  <1>13 [req] Applying to a one-wire actual rejects.
  <1>14 [req] Applying the same template to distinct frame slices cannot change its captured outer binding.
ISA-11.1-P15
  <1>1 [req] **Closure-bound argument vectors (K24).** An `arguments` formal's `symbol` names an earlier symbol formal.   deps: ISA-0.2-P24.S1 (K24)
  <1>2 [req] Its actual is an array of actuals in that linked symbol's own formal order, or a bound arguments name for the identical closure hash.   deps: ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash)
  <1>3 [req] The vector captures the actuals' lexical environment; validate every argument against that symbol's interface at binding, even when no call executes.
  <1>4 [req] `call` additionally accepts the bound arguments name as its `args` value; it forwards the whole vector without reinterpreting names in the forwarding recipe's scope.
  <1>5 [req] Reject a vector bound to another closure, even if the two interfaces have equal widths.
  <1>6 [req] Each call still has its own invocation path and callee ordinals.
ISA-11.1-P16
  <1>1 [req] A `value` formal's `isa_type` is `iN` (N a canonical positive decimal), `f64`, `token`, or exactly `{ "ptr": N }` with N the linked object's positive `ptr_width`.
  <1>2 [req] Values use exactly §12.3 invocation encodings: canonical unsigned decimal strings within the iN/ptr range, binary64 bit-pattern hex (including signed zero and NaN payloads for classical f64), or four canonical nonnegative decimal strings for a token tuple.   deps: ISA-12.3-P2.S2 (unsigned decimal strings), ISA-12.3
  <1>3 [req] A value may also be the name of a bound value of exactly the same `isa_type`; widening or converting it implicitly is forbidden.
  <1>4 [req] Token values carry tuples, never process-local ids.
  <1>5 [req] This adds no arithmetic on classical f64 or tokens inside a recipe.
ISA-11.1-P17
  <1>1 [req] Witness: a linked interface `(wire[1], wire[3], i128, f64, token)` accepts `[a,b,"18446744073709551617","0x7ff8000000000001",["2","0","0","0"]]`.
  <1>2 [req] A forwarding recipe `(S:symbol,A:arguments(S))` with two calls using A preserves all bindings and emits two distinct invocation paths starting at ordinal zero.
  <1>3 [req] Swapping a and b rejects; 2^128 for i128, a three-part token, or an arguments vector for a different closure rejects.
  <1>4 [req] This binding rule does not replace §3.3's source interface region shape/role validation or the §9.2 linker obligation that the registry interface and body match the supplied closure hash.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region), ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash), ISA-3.3, ISA-9.2
ISA-11.1-P18
  <1>1 [req] **Ordered descriptors (K25).** A `records` formal has exactly `name`, `type`, `fields`, `order`.   deps: ISA-0.2-P25.S1 (K25)
  <1>2 [req] `fields` is an ordered typed formal list; its types are `integer`, `wire`, `region`, `class`, `allocation`, or `image` (K21, for source data-image descriptors).   deps: ISA-0.2-P21.S1 (K21)
  <1>3 [req] A field's width/shape expressions may use enclosing earlier integer formals and earlier integer fields, never later fields.
  <1>4 [req] `order` is an array of distinct integer field names.
  <1>5 [req] An actual is an array of objects whose keys are exactly the field names, or a bound records name with the identical field schema and order.
  <1>6 [req] Each field actual resolves in the actual's captured lexical environment; earlier integer fields additionally bind width/shape expressions.
  <1>7 [req] Forwarding preserves resolved identities, revalidates widths against the receiving enclosing integers, and never resolves a wire name again.
  <1>8 [req] With nonempty `order`, the tuples of those fields must be strictly increasing lexicographically; with empty `order`, array order is normative.
  <1>9 [req] Empty arrays are valid.
  <1>10 [req] `{"visit": name, "body": [...]}` visits records in array order, binding all their fields lexically for that body, shadowing outer names across all types and restoring them afterwards.
  <1>11 [req] An inverse visits the same records in reverse order and adjoints each body.
  <1>12 [req] No record iteration emits a leaf by itself.
  <1>13 [req] Reject extra/missing fields, an invalid schema, order mismatch, duplicate/out-of-order keys, or any invalid field actual, even for an unvisited list.
  <1>14 [req] Record iteration and K22 template application share the same capture, effective-control and occurrence-identity rules.   deps: ISA-0.2-P22.S1 (K22)
ISA-11.1-P19
  <1>1 [unc] The following descriptor schemas bind source metadata, rather than caller-supplied circuit bodies.
  <1>2 [req] Source formation MUST check every record against the linked source declarations/layout before expansion:
ISA-11.1-P20
  <1>1 [req] A byte-layout list has integer fields `base`, `extent`, then `cells:wire[8*extent]`, ordered by `base`.
  <1>2 [req] Bases/extents are nonnegative mathematical integers, extents positive.
  <1>3 [req] Form the list by partitioning `[0,2^H)` at every mapped interface region endpoint.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region)
  <1>4 [req] Each mapped interval binds its region's cells, flattened by increasing cell index and then LSB-first bit index.
  <1>5 [req] Each remaining maximal interval binds the same address slice of ordinary `mem`.
  <1>6 [req] Adjacent mapped intervals remain separate records.
  <1>7 [req] Require exact coverage, no overlap and no gap; mapped cells have W=8 as §7.2 requires.   deps: ISA-7.2
  <1>8 [req] Byte `base+j` is exactly wires `cells[8*j .. 8*j+8)`.
  <1>9 [req] Ordinary `mem` under a mapped interval is never selected by an access.
  <1>10 [req] Interface shape and origin use K26.   deps: ISA-0.2-P26.S1 (K26)
ISA-11.1-P21
  <1>1 [req] An activation location list has `id`, `width`, `slot` integers followed by `location:wire[width]`, ordered by `id`.
  <1>2 [req] The ids and widths are exactly §3.7's global ids and declared widths.   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>3 [req] For a selected instance, `slot` is its zero-based declaration index in L_f, including parameters and the result name; for a closure delivery list it is the declaration index within the location's own instance.   deps: ISA-3.7-P10.S1 (result name)
  <1>4 [req] A capture list has `position`, `width` integers followed by `source:wire[width]`, `parameter:wire[width]`, `capture:wire[width]`, ordered by `position`.
  <1>5 [req] Positions are 0..m-1 in source argument order; parameter and capture are the corresponding callee location and frame displacement slot.
  <1>6 [req] The stack low-width slice for location slot j starts at `(old_sp+3+j)*W_cell` in C2 and `(restored_fp+3+j)*W_cell` in R3; higher cell bits are untouched.   deps: ISA-3.7-P13.S1 (C2), ISA-3.7-P21.S1 (R3)
  <1>7 [req] Delivery scans the complete closure list in global id order.
  <1>8 [req] Reject records inconsistent with those source mappings.
  <1>9 [req] These lists add no locations for token/classical/region arguments; those remain K24/K26 fixed bindings.   deps: ISA-0.2-P24.S1 (K24), ISA-0.2-P26.S1 (K26)
ISA-11.1-P22
  <1>1 [req] A construction state list has `width`, `initial` integers, `I:image`, `storage:allocation`, with empty `order` (array order).
  <1>2 [req] Each storage identity is distinct, clean and exactly `width` bits; its class is `any` for the implicit §5.2 state.   deps: ISA-5.2
  <1>3 [req] In allocation order the list is: pc, pc-prime, faulted, code, result_val; then heap_used, atop, sp, fp, depth, ordinary mem, stack when layout state exists; locations in global id order; local regions in declaration order; alloctab when layout state exists; frames 0..B-1.
  <1>4 [req] Omit only the layout state excluded by §7.2's memoryless-object rule.   deps: ISA-7.2
  <1>5 [req] Counter widths/values are the reference widths and initial values of §3.7; all other non-image initial values are zero except alloctab pre-entries.   deps: ISA-3.7   declared: ISA-3.7
  <1>6 [req] A table cell packs base[ptr_width], size[ptr_width], live, pinned, in that LSB-first order; pre-entries are in §3.7's layout order and unused entries are zero.   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>7 [req] Image-bearing mem and local regions have initial=0.
  <1>8 [req] Ordinary mem's I is exactly 2^H bytes, initially zero, with each global's image overlaid at its layout base; bytes hidden by mapped interface regions remain zero.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region)
  <1>9 [req] A local region's I is its exact bound data image, or empty for absent/default zero.
  <1>10 [req] Every other record has I empty and initial the nonnegative integer encoding of its initial bit vector, strictly below 2^width.
  <1>11 [req] Mapped interface regions are borrowed inputs, never acquired by this state list.   deps: ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region)
  <1>12 [req] A separate frame list has `index` integer and `frame:allocation`, ordered by index; it contains exactly B entries indexed 0..B-1, referring to the identical frame handles in the state list, each with §3.6's fixed frame width and field order.   deps: ISA-3.6   declared: ISA-3.6
  <1>13 [req] Reject any mismatch of source state, image, width, class, order or identity.
  <1>14 [req] The construction acquires/initializes this list, invokes the catalog `step` once per frame, commits, then reverses frames and initialization before releasing the list in reverse order; source row/successor templates supply rows, never a replacement dispatcher or construction body.   declared: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-3.6, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P22
ISA-11.1-P23
  <1>1 [req] Witness: byte records `(base=0,extent=1,cells=a[8])` and `(base=1,extent=2,cells=b[16])` bind addresses 0,1,2 to a[0..8), b[0..8),b[8..16), respectively.
  <1>2 [req] Visiting X on each first bit emits X a[0], X b[0]; inverse emits X b[0], X a[0].
  <1>3 [req] A two-bit location id 0/slot 0 followed by a one-bit location id 1/slot 1 exchanges only the low 2 and low 1 bits of stack cells old_sp+3 and old_sp+4; return/inverse address them at restored_fp+3 and restored_fp+4.
  <1>4 [req] Exchanging the two record positions rejects for id order.
  <1>5 [req] A construction witness with rows `loc = lift.add x y; lift.retval loc` commits fault 1 for B=0/1 and `(x+y) mod 2^W` into the arbitrary target for B>=2, preserving inputs and cleaning every state/frame allocation.
  <1>6 [req] A local image 81 emits unconditional X at cell bits 0,7 and restores them before release, including under a false external predicate.
ISA-11.1-P24
  <1>1 [req] **Source region bindings (K26).** A `region` formal has exactly `name`, `type`, `address`, `cell`, `role`; address/cell are integer expressions using earlier integers, with A>=0, W>0, and role is `in` or `mut`.   deps: ISA-0.2-P26.S1 (K26)
  <1>2 [req] Its actual is a bound region name or exactly `{"address": A, "cell": W, "role": role, "origin": origin, "cells": actual}`.
  <1>3 [req] A and W in the actual are nonnegative/positive integer literals, role must equal the formal role, and the cell wire actual has exactly 2^A*W wires, in cell-index/LSB-first order.
  <1>4 [req] Both shape components must equal the formal components, even if a different shape has the same total width.
  <1>5 [req] Origin is `interface:` followed by the canonical source operand name for a caller-owned interface, or `local:` followed by the local source declaration's NAME token.
  <1>6 [req] Each suffix is the exact canonical §16.1 NAME token, without a sigil.   deps: ISA-16.1
  <1>7 [req] Its binding together with the ordered cell wire identities identifies the source occurrence (including repeated invocations); forwarding preserves both.
  <1>8 [req] The suffix is checked against the linked source binding; a local origin cannot be substituted for an interface origin by changing its string.
  <1>9 [req] A region formal can be used as its flattened cells in any wire actual, while forwarding it as a region retains shape, role, origin and wire identities.
  <1>10 [req] K24 argument vectors validate regions in the linked signature's slot order just as wires/values; source role/overlap restrictions remain §3.3.   deps: ISA-0.2-P24.S1 (K24), ISA-3.3
  <1>11 [req] Lifted calls accept only their permitted mapped/parameter origins (§4.2); action calls may accept local regions where §3.3 permits them.   deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-3.3
  <1>12 [req] No rule infers source shape or role from total bit width.
  <1>13 [req] Witness: a region[1,3] in local:r over six wires forwards unchanged through two K24 calls.   deps: ISA-0.2-P24.S1 (K24)
  <1>14 [req] Binding it to region[0,6] in or region[1,3] mut rejects, despite equal total width.
  <1>15 [req] Invalid/missing origins also reject.
ISA-11.1-P25
  <1>1 [req] **Classes and allocation declarations (K27).** A `class` formal has exactly `name`,`type`.   deps: ISA-0.2-P27.S1 (K27)
  <1>2 [req] A class actual is a bound class name or exactly `{"class": text}`, where text is the exact source class spelling (`any` included), never an empty string or a resolved substitute class.
  <1>3 [req] `{"declare": name, "width": scalar, "class": actual, "state": state, "body": [...]}` reserves a fresh allocation identity with nonnegative width, that class, and state `clean` or `borrowed`.
  <1>4 [req] It emits no leaf; it binds an initially inactive allocation declaration for its lexical body, including captured actual bodies.
  <1>5 [req] It may not shadow any binding.
  <1>6 [req] An `allocation` formal (exactly `name`,`type`) passes that declaration by identity, including metadata and live/inactive status.
  <1>7 [req] Exactly `{"acquire": name}` emits its unconditional allocation and changes it from inactive to live; exactly `{"relinquish": name}` emits its matching unconditional release and changes it from live to inactive.
  <1>8 [req] An acquire requires inactive, a relinquish requires live, and the declaration must be inactive when its lexical body exits.
  <1>9 [req] While live, its name is a wire actual of its declared width, including for subsequent caller constructors after `invoke` returns; while inactive any wire use rejects.
  <1>10 [req] Captures and invocations share the same status, not a copy of the lifetime.
  <1>11 [req] Inverse exchanges acquire/relinquish, using the same declaration metadata and occurrence identity.
  <1>12 [req] Reacquisition denotes a new allocation occurrence, with fresh identity even within one declaration.
  <1>13 [req] Clean release requires zero; borrowed release requires restoration on the full domain (§5.6), never zero.   deps: ISA-5.6
  <1>14 [req] These are user/instruction allocation results; internal library scratch still obeys the clean LIFO rule below.   deps: ISA-5.5-P1.S5 (LIFO)
  <1>15 [req] A declaration or allocation formal listed in `scratch` when acquired marks that entire allocation lifetime as library scratch; the mark follows its identity through captures and invokes, requires clean state and cannot be removed by releasing through a different formal name.
  <1>16 [req] Allocations are unconditional even under a false inherited control.
  <1>17 [req] Witness: declare out[2] of class ancilla; invoke an allocation recipe on out; apply two X gates to out[1] in separate caller/callee bodies; invoke its release recipe.
  <1>18 [req] All leaves refer to the one live allocation identity and release it clean.
  <1>19 [req] Wire use before acquisition/after release, double acquisition, unmatched release or leaving the declaration live rejects.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (Wire)
  <1>20 [req] A borrowed declaration preserves the exact class and both initial values of every borrowed bit; no conversion to class any is permitted.
ISA-11.1-P26
  <1>1 [req] **Angles and quantum witnesses (K20).** An `angle` actual is a bound angle formal or a finite binary64 value.   deps: ISA-0.2-P20.S1 (K20)
  <1>2 [req] Reusing §12.3's bit-pattern encoding: `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16 lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost).   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>3 [req] Angles additionally reject NaN and infinities (§3.1).   deps: ISA-3.1
  <1>4 [req] Only `rx`, `ry`, `rz` have `params`, an array containing exactly one angle; other §4.4 gates have no angle parameters.   deps: ISA-4.4
  <1>5 [req] No recipe arithmetic on angles is permitted.
  <1>6 [req] `inverse` negates each rotation angle by toggling bit 63, including signed zero, and exchanges s/sdg and t/tdg as §5.3 requires.   deps: ISA-5.3
ISA-11.1-P27
  <1>1 [req] For h, phase gates s/sdg/t/tdg/cz, and rotations, correctness is equality of the §14.2 unitary (using §4.4 matrices), column by column on the full computational basis of all recipe interface wires, with scratch initially clean and finally clean.   deps: ISA-14.2, ISA-4.4
  <1>2 [req] Arbitrary targets, controls and borrowed inputs are included.
  <1>3 [req] This full-basis unitary is the witness extending T3's basis argument; non-permutation leaves MUST NOT be certified by a basis permutation simulator.   deps: ISA-14.3-P3.S1 (T3)
  <1>4 [req] Exact algebraic amplitude checks apply to Clifford+T leaves.
  <1>5 [req] For arbitrary binary64 angles a numerical checker must state its round-trip criterion and error bounds; numerical agreement is evidence, not a proof of ε=0 for arbitrary transcendental matrix entries.
ISA-11.1-P28
  <1>1 [req] **REF-BINDING / REF-QUANTUM witnesses.** With formals `[{"name":"N","type":"integer"},{"name":"w","type":"wire","width":"N"}, {"name":"theta","type":"angle"},{"name":"B","type":"body"}]`, actuals `[1,{"range":"a","from":0,"to":1},"0x8000000000000000",[]]` bind w[0]=a[0], θ=−0 and an empty body.
  <1>2 [req] Replacing the range end by 0 rejects for width mismatch.
  <1>3 [req] Splicing a body allocating/releasing `tmp` twice gives two distinct scratch identities even if the callee also declares `tmp`.
  <1>4 [req] An inverse rx at `0x3ff0000000000000` emits rx at `0xbff0000000000000`.
  <1>5 [req] For `w=[a[0],a[1]]` and t=a[2], `{"gate":"mcx","wires":["w","t"]}` emits exactly those three operands; `each` over w visits a[0] then a[1].
  <1>6 [req] Two calls to the same closure have separate invocation paths, each starting ordinal zero.
  <1>7 [req] H's two columns are (1,1)/√2 and (1,−1)/√2; S's second column is (0,i), distinguishing these witnesses from a bit permutation and fixing global phase.
ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>1 [req] Number every emitted leaf (including allocation/release and classical leaves) 0,1,… within the containing row/instruction, after instantiating widths and static loop counts, before any §5.7 transformation or target scheme expansion.   deps: ISA-5.7
  <1>2 [req] Calls have their own invocation-path element and callee ordinal space; `step` and `phase` select the invocation's oracle step as in §11.   deps: ISA-11
  <1>3 [req] The inverse of a row visits the forward ordinals in reverse order, retaining each ordinal under `phase: rev`; it does not renumber them.
  <1>4 [req] Scratch identity is `(invocation chain, step, recipe expansion path, allocation ordinal, bit index)`; the chain includes fixed-binding instance and semantic-call occurrence, and allocations in repeated bodies have their expanded occurrence path.   deps: ISA-11-P5.S1 (invocation chain)   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>5 [req] All library scratch is clean, allocated unconditionally and released in reverse allocation order after uncomputation; borrowed user operands remain borrowed.
  <1>6 [req] `removable` is an explicit array of leaf ordinals and MUST be empty for every recipe in `ref` version 0.9.
  <1>7 [req] No transformation may infer extra permission from a zero-valued basis input.
  <1>8 [req] Motivation (0.8 §11): "MUST keep the ordinals of the operations they keep and MAY remove only operations the library marks removable"; §17 item 7: "clean scratch released in reverse order".   deps: ISA-17-P8
ISA-11.1-P30
  <1>1 [req] The reverse-allocation release rule governs recipe scratch; an instruction whose own semantics fix a release sequence (qinplace per §4.5: `qfree t; qfree f`) keeps that sequence, and its recipe MUST reproduce it exactly.   deps: ~ISA-4.5   declared: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11.1-P29.S5
ISA-11.1-P31
  <1>1 [req] **Instruction-release witness (K28).** This is a synthetic constructor-binding witness whose two callee fixtures each contain one CX at callee ordinal 0; it illustrates caller ordinals and release order, rather than the complete `ref` expansion or cost of an ISA oracle.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P28.S1 (K28)   declared: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P22, ISA-11.1-P29.S1, ISA-11.1-P29.S2
  <1>2 [req] For n=1, F=4 the private instruction trace is alloc t[1] at row ordinal 0, alloc f[4] at 1, call G, swap x/t at 2, call H, release t at 3, release f at 4, with each call's complete expansion in its own ordinal space.   declared: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-5.4, ISA-11.1-P29.S1, ISA-11.1-P29.S2
  <1>3 [req] The certified pair leaves t/f clean for both x values and both effective controls; allocations and releases are unconditional.   declared: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-3.5, ISA-4.3
  <1>4 [req] Recipe-internal scratch within either call still releases in reverse allocation order.   declared: ISA-11.1-P29.S5
  <1>5 [req] Raw inverse traversal retains forward ordinals, reverses their order and adjoints the leaves; it describes reversal of an already expanded machine row, rather than formation of a scheduled action adjoint.   declared: ISA-5.3, ISA-11.1-P29.S3
ISA-11.1-P32
  <1>1 [req] **Scheduled action adjoint (K62).** Before recipe instantiation, form an action adjoint using the §5.3 instruction replacements in reverse source order, and bind the `adj` recipe's body B to that source-adjointed body; `adj` splices B without applying a leaf inverse.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P62.S1 (K62), ~ISA-5.3   declared: ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>2 [req] Each replacement instruction has its own forward ordinal space, so `adj(qinplace G H)` instantiates `qinplace H G` with allocations t then f, exchange, and releases t then f in caller ordinals 0,1,2,3,4 for n=1, F=4; its calls are H then G, each with a separate callee space.   declared: ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11.1-P29.S1, ISA-11.1-P29.S2
  <1>3 [req] The `qif` replacement still reverses its complete §4.6 expansion, including unconditional lifetimes.   deps: ~ISA-4.6   declared: ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-4.6-P7
  <1>4 [req] Schedulers, executors and estimators use this same action-adjoint trace; raw `inverse` constructors retain forward ordinals when reversing an already expanded row, including inverse oracle steps.   declared: ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P29.S3, ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.3-P4
  <1>5 [req] For an alloc entry at this instruction with `generated: 0` and class `special`, with other allocations resolving to class `default`, the private allocations are t[1]:special and f[4]:default.   declared: ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6, ISA-4.5-P5
ISA-11.1-P33
  <1>1 [req] **Decomposition set.** The §17 item 7 defaults are made explicit below.   deps: ISA-17-P8
  <1>2 [req] Bit loops below increase from the least-significant bit.
  <1>3 [req] To make Boolean recipes deterministic, use no common-subexpression elimination: visit every Boolean expression tree in left-to-right postorder, giving each internal node a fresh clean scratch bit.
  <1>4 [req] NOT(a) uses X d; CX a d, XOR(a,b) uses CX a d; CX b d, AND(a,b) uses CCX a b d.
  <1>5 [req] OR(a,b) is the ordered tree XOR(XOR(a,b),AND(a,b)); repeated operands are separate tree occurrences (coincident AND operands reduce to a single CX so the gate has distinct operands).
  <1>6 [req] XOR the root into the destination, then reverse all compute gates and release scratch in reverse allocation order.
  <1>7 [req] Constants are zero or X of a clean bit.
  <1>8 [req] All formulas use old source values captured before an assignment/exchange.
  <1>9 [req] External effective controls apply to the resulting semantic gates, not allocations; scratch computations may be unconditional provided their compute/uncompute pair is balanced when the external control is false (§3.5, §4.3).   deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.3
ISA-11.1-P34
  <1>1 [req] Copy, XOR, exchange; native gates | `qxor`: CX s[i] t[i]; `qcopy`: allocate then the same; `quncopy`: the same then release; `qexch`: SWAP a[i] b[i].
  <1>2 [req] Keep each §4.4 gate as a leaf; target schemes expand later. | §4.5: "`cx s[i] t[i]`" and "`swap a[i] b[i]`"; §8.3: "Every template gate MUST itself match a gates entry".   deps: ISA-4.4, ISA-4.5, ISA-8.3
ISA-11.1-P35
  <1>1 [req] Ripple add/sub and comparisons | c[0]=0; p[i]=a[i] XOR b[i]; sum[i]=p[i] XOR c[i]; c[i+1]=(a[i] AND b[i]) XOR (p[i] AND c[i]); compute each bit in this order, XOR sums out, reverse scratch.
  <1>2 [req] Subtraction adds bitwise NOT b with c[0]=1.
  <1>3 [req] Unsigned a<b is NOT the final subtraction carry; equality is the AND of NOT(a[i] XOR b[i]) in increasing-bit left-associated order.
  <1>4 [req] `ule` is OR(ult,eq); signed comparison selects sign(a) if signs differ, else ult. | §17 item 7: "ripple-carry adders and comparators"; §4.1: "wrap"; §3.7: prospective sums use "no wrap" (retain the extra carry for those).   deps: ISA-17-P8, ISA-4.1, ISA-3.7
ISA-11.1-P36
  <1>1 [req] Other arithmetic, casts, select | AND/XOR/OR use the Boolean trees above; select emits ((NOT p) AND b[i]) XOR (p AND a[i]).
  <1>2 [req] Multiply folds shifted partial products in increasing multiplier-bit order through ripple add modulo 2^N.
  <1>3 [req] Shifts scan shift amounts j=0..N−1, equality-selecting the corresponding source bit; ≥N produces zero.
  <1>4 [req] Sign-extend replicates the high source bit, zero-extend supplies zero, truncate drops high bits.
  <1>5 [req] Division uses restoring unsigned long division, scanning dividend bits from N−1 to 0: append that bit to an N+1-bit remainder, compare with the zero-extended divisor, subtract iff ≥, and emit that predicate as quotient bit.
  <1>6 [req] Signed forms conditionally negate operands, divide magnitudes, negate quotient iff signs differ and remainder iff dividend negative; row fault predicates precede these outputs. | §4.2: "w (under ¬flag)" and the div fault predicates; §4.1 wrap/shift rules and signed MIN/−1 fault; §3.3 equal operand widths.   deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.1, ISA-3.3
ISA-11.1-P37
  <1>1 [req] Linear inrange and flags | For table entries i=0..2^A_tab−1 compute `(i<atop) AND live_i AND (base_i≤p) AND (p+n≤base_i+size_i)` in that parenthesisation at §4.2's widened width; left-fold OR from false, then uncompute entry scratch.   deps: ISA-4.2
  <1>2 [req] `free` and allocator/call fault trees use their explicit §4.2/§3.7 formula and slot order (including 0.9's E17.13 correction). | §4.2: "inrange(p,n) = ∃ i < atop ..." and "no wrap"; §17 item 7: "linear inrange scans".   deps: ISA-0.1-P39.S1 (E17.13), ISA-4.2, ISA-3.7, ISA-17-P8
ISA-11.1-P38
  <1>1 [req] Address dispatch and multiplexers | Scan every address i in increasing numeric order; compute equality to i, copy/exchange cell bits in increasing order under that bit, uncompute equality before the next i.
  <1>2 [req] Lifted memory chooses the §7.2 mapped region in address order and scans its cells in increasing address order; captures use old addresses before exchanged counters. | §4.6: "for each cell i: under a == i"; §17 item 7: "one-hot address dispatch"; §3.7 C1–C5/R1–R5.   deps: ISA-3.7-P12.S1 (C1), ISA-3.7-P13.S1 (C2), ISA-3.7-P14.S1 (C3), ISA-3.7-P15.S1 (C4), ISA-3.7-P16.S1 (C5), ISA-3.7-P19.S1 (R1), ISA-3.7-P20.S1 (R2), ISA-3.7-P21.S1 (R3), ISA-3.7-P22.S1 (R4), ISA-3.7-P23.S1 (R5), ISA-7.2, ISA-4.6, ISA-17-P8, ISA-3.7
ISA-11.1-P39
  <1>1 [req] Row, oracle and structure | Compose these recipes in the printed §3.6 step order, §4.2 slot order, §3.7 activation order, §5.2 allocate/forward/commit/reverse/release order.   deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-4.2, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.2
  <1>2 [req] `qif`, loops, local-region image X gates and `qinplace` use the literal sequences of §4.5–§4.6, incorporating 0.9's explicit parameters.   deps: ISA-4.5, ISA-4.6
  <1>3 [req] Cleanup `reverse` adjoints that sequence; `recompute` follows §6.4. | §11: "Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6 and §5.2"; §5.2: "run the inverse step for t = B..1, then release (1)".   deps: ISA-6.4, ISA-11, ISA-3.6, ISA-4.2, ISA-4.5, ISA-4.6, ISA-5.2
ISA-11.1-P40
  <1>1 [req] The permutation algorithms define exact permutation circuits, preserving every input required by the row and cleaning scratch; circuit correctness must still be checked against full-domain T3 witnesses and §5.7, not inferred from costs or names.   deps: ISA-14.3-P3.S1 (T3), ISA-5.7
  <1>2 [req] Complete draft catalog: **219 recipes**; envelope SHA-256 `5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`.   declared: ISA-11.1-P2, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P28, ISA-11.1-P29
  <1>3 [req] Publication follows acceptance.   declared: ISA-11.1-P1, ISA-11.1-P2
  <1>4 [req] In particular this is not permission to publish a partial library that omits division, stack, coherent control, memory or any instruction.
ISA-11.1-P41

  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"isa":"0.9","name":"ref","recipes":[{"kind":"qxor","operations":[{"gate":"cx","…
ISA-11.1-P42
````
