### §12.1 Claims   [ISA-12.1]

````text
ISA-12.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] `exhaustive` | `domain` (§12.2); `expected`: `"oracle"` \| `{ "table": [...] }` \| `{ "program": closure_hash }` \| `"inverse_pair"` | re-execute every domain element on the reference interpreter / expected program; compare full tuples.   deps: ISA-12.2
  <1>2 [req] For `"inverse_pair"` (the claim of an `inverse_pairs` entry, §12.3): for every element (x, ins, mem cells, args) run g and ginv at the entry's tuple, token-typed arguments at their `arg_tokens` tuples, and require both fault-free, ginv(g(x)) = x and g(ginv(x)) = x; the bundles and regions components MUST be the full domain of g's `in` and `mem` slots (the `args` component lists the covered classical values)   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle), ISA-12.3
ISA-12.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] `sampled` | `domain`; `expected`: `"oracle"` \| `{ "table": [...] }` \| `{ "program": closure_hash }`; `subject` and invocation binding as below (K13); `sampler`: `{ "algorithm": "xoshiro256**", "seed": [4 decimal strings, not all zero], "count": n }` | independently replay both sides of the declared relation on every sampled element; compare full tuples; always **evidence**, even if every element is drawn   deps: ISA-0.2-P13.S1 (K13)
ISA-12.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] `structural` | `{ "rule": "translation_lemma" \| "inverse_pair_library" \| "adjoint_table", "subclaims": [indices], "witness": … }`; for `translation_lemma`: `{ "V": [points], "W": [points], "X": [wires], "B": [wires], "frame": [first point, last point] }`; for `inverse_pair_library`: `{ "library_closure_hash", "claim_index" }`; for `adjoint_table`: `{}` | check the rule's side conditions syntactically against the object and the subclaims' results
ISA-12.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] `numerical` | `{ "subject": { "closure_hash", "variant", "inherited_controls", "schedule_hash" }, "metric": "operator_norm", "precision_bits": n, "bound": decimal }` | **checker-computed, for every invocation context of the subject** (below): build the realised unitary U of that invocation (the region's realisation under the root schedule at that path, with its resolved classical arguments, budget, epsilon shares and cleanup choices, and `inherited_controls` control wires prepended) and the reference unitary V (the region's §5.3 semantics with the same arguments, controlled on the same wires) at the subject's signature widths in interval arithmetic at `precision_bits` (basis order = allocation order, controls first), form interval enclosures of `V†U − I` entrywise, derive the operator-norm bound as the Frobenius-norm upper bound in interval arithmetic, and verify it is ≤ `bound`; the claim holds iff it holds for every context   deps: ISA-5.3
ISA-12.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] `channel` | `{ "subject": as above, "metric": "diamond", "precision_bits", "bound", "dual": [ { "Y": interval Hermitian matrix, "mu": decimal } … ] }` | for every invocation context of the subject, in enumeration order, verify its dual witness for the **diamond-norm SDP** below in interval arithmetic and `mu` ≤ `bound` (one witness per context; too few witnesses: reject)
ISA-12.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] **CLAIM-REPLAY** (K13; b-isa-spec-9uy, from bennett-vm-clw). Sampled observations remain evidence under the following replay contract.   deps: ISA-0.2-P13.S1 (K13)
ISA-12.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] **Comparison relation.** `exhaustive` and `sampled` claims MUST name their `subject: { closure_hash, variant, inherited_controls, schedule_hash? }` and an explicit `expected`; there is no implicit self-comparison or default expected relation.
  <1>2 [req] Without a schedule the left side is the subject's reference semantics at the bound invocation; with one it is that invocation's realised operation.
  <1>3 [req] `expected: "oracle"` compares the realised operation to §5.2's reference oracle map and requires `schedule_hash`.   deps: ISA-5.2   declared: ISA-5.2, ISA-11, ISA-12.1-P11
  <1>4 [req] `expected: {program: H}` independently runs the reference semantics of linked closure H at the **same** arguments, budget and interface tuple; its signature MUST be compatible.
  <1>5 [req] `expected: {table: rows}` compares to the explicit full-tuple rows below.
  <1>6 [req] The checker MUST compare every interface output, preserve inputs and borrowed systems, and verify clean workspace and exact +1 basis phase for a permutation assertion (T3); matching a target or fault projection alone, or a producer's claimed verdict, is insufficient.   deps: ISA-14.3-P3.S1 (T3)   declared: ISA-14.3-P3, ISA-5.6
  <1>7 [req] For a non-permutation realisation, use the existing numerical/channel claims rather than labeling basis observations a permutation proof.   declared: ISA-12.1-P4, ISA-12.1-P5, ISA-14.2
  <1>8 [req] K4's `expected: "inverse_pair"` retains its separate two-composition relation and entry/domain bindings; it is not a sampled proof option.   deps: ISA-0.2-P4.S1 (K4)
ISA-12.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] **Table tuples.** Each table row is `{ before: tuple, after: tuple }`.
  <1>2 [req] A tuple is `{ bundles: ["<unsigned integer>", …], borrowed: ["<unsigned integer>", …], regions: [["<cell>", …], …] }`.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>3 [req] `bundles` lists inherited control wires first (each width 1), then all signature bundles in operand order, **including arbitrary initial oracle target and fault bundles**.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>4 [req] `borrowed` lists dirty bundles in allocation order, with widths fixed by the checked borrow lifetimes; `regions` lists interface regions in signature order, every cell in increasing address order, each as an unsigned W-bit integer.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle), ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region)
  <1>5 [req] All integers use canonical decimal strings, within the declared widths; no cells, inputs or unchanged components may be omitted.
  <1>6 [req] Workspace is not an interface component and MUST finish zero.
  <1>7 [req] Rows are in domain enumeration order, one per distinct declared domain element (including for `sampled`); duplicate/missing/out-of-range rows reject.
  <1>8 [req] This favors an explicit table; `{program: H}` provides the compact relation when a full table is large.
  <1>9 [req] Sampling draws with replacement using the existing sampler; coverage counts distinct drawn elements, not draws.
  <1>10 [req] A mismatch rejects; success is always `evidence` with that distinct covered fraction of D, including when the draws happen to cover all of D.
ISA-12.1-P9
  <1>1 [unc] **CLAIM-REPLAY witness**   deps: ISA-12.1-P6.S1 (CLAIM-REPLAY)
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 18 lines) {
ISA-12.1-P10
  <1>1 [req] The subject is a two-row oracle body `r = lift.add x a;` `lift.retval r`, widths x = target = a = 2 and fault = 4, with a preserved one-bit dirty bundle and an unused two-cell, eight-bit `in` interface region; no inherited controls.   deps: ~ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body), ~ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)   declared: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.2, ISA-12.1-P8
  <1>2 [req] A legal layout has ptr_width=2, H=2, A_tab=1, S_cells=0, null_size=1, no globals, the interface mapped at base 1 with extent 2, heap_base=3 and heap_limit_max=0; the token is (2,0,0,0), and the unused mapping is retained even though the body has no load/store.   declared: ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2, ISA-5.4
  <1>3 [req] The **body** does not access memory; the interface cells remain part of D.   declared: ISA-7.2, ISA-12.2
  <1>4 [req] The reference payload is (1 + 2) mod 4 = 3: target 2 XOR 3 = 1 and fault 3 XOR 0 = 3.
  <1>5 [req] The singleton declared domain is precisely `row.before`, within the full 25-bit D (2+2+4+1+16 bits).   declared: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.2, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2
  <1>6 [req] Four samples of singleton components draw no generator words and cover one of 33554432 full tuples.   declared: ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.1-P13, ISA-12.2
  <1>7 [req] An identity implementation would agree with itself but MUST fail against `row`; using `wrong_row` also MUST fail.
  <1>8 [req] At argument 1 the target is 2 XOR 2 = 0; at B = 1 the payload is zero and fault is 3 XOR 1 = 2.
  <1>9 [req] A certificate for argument 2, B = 2 cannot be replayed as either of those invocations.
  <1>10 [req] The printed rows illustrate the relation; bound closure/object hashes still have to be supplied in an actual certificate, not invented for this witness.
ISA-12.1-P11
  <1>1 [req] **Invocation contexts.** A subject denotes the set of invocations of the region `closure_hash`, with the given `variant` and `inherited_ controls`, in the root schedule `schedule_hash`: every invocation of the region in the root realisation's complete invocation tree — including invocations that no schedule entry names and defaults alone cover (§11) — with the classical arguments, budget, `epsilon_share`s and cleanup realisations resolved for it.   deps: ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] An invocation's **context key** is its concrete invocation chain (§11), outermost first, each element `{ "symbol", "point", "iteration", "instance", "call_index" }` with every iteration index, fixed-binding key and forward semantic-call occurrence resolved and, for an element inside an oracle, `"step"` (1..B) and `"phase"` (`"fwd"` or `"rev"`) added; contexts are enumerated in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encodings of their keys (wildcard selectors play no part).   deps: ISA-11-P5.S1 (invocation chain), ISA-11   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-11, ISA-11.1-P9
  <1>3 [req] Every claim bound and every `error` entry (§12.3) is a **common upper bound over all contexts** of its key; the multiplicity composition of §12.3 is sound for that reason.   deps: ISA-12.3
ISA-12.1-P12
  <1>1 [def] **Diamond-norm SDP** (Watrous, *Theory of Computing* 5 (2009), §4; the Hermiticity-preserving simplification).   deps: ISA-4
  <1>2 [req] Let Φ = Φ_real − Φ_ref be the difference of the subject's realised channel and the reference channel (both on the subject's interface, controls included), d_in = d_out = d the dimension.
  <1>3 [def] The **Choi matrix** is J(Φ) = Σ_{i,j} |i⟩⟨j| ⊗ Φ(|i⟩⟨j|) (input factor first, output factor second, computational basis in allocation order, **unnormalised**).
  <1>4 [req] The witness is a Hermitian Y of dimension d² and a decimal mu.
  <1>5 [req] The checker accepts iff, with every matrix entry an interval and every test rigorous (an inconclusive test rejects): Y − J(Φ) ⪰ 0, Y + J(Φ) ⪰ 0, mu·I − Tr_out(Y) ⪰ 0 (partial trace over the output factor), and mu ≤ `bound`; then ‖Φ‖◇ ≤ mu (the dual of Watrous's SDP with Y₀ = Y₁ = Y; this is the diamond norm itself, not half of it).
  <1>6 [non] *(non-normative)* With this input-first Choi convention Tr_out traces the second factor, Tr[(ρ ⊗ I)Y] = Tr[ρ Tr_out(Y)]; a primal formulation that labels the input factor ρᵀ ranges over the same set of density matrices, so the bound is unchanged.
  <1>7 [req] Encoding: `Y` is a row-major array of entries `[re_lo, re_ hi, im_lo, im_hi]` (decimal strings).
  <1>8 [req] **Positivity test.** The checker proves each of the three matrices **positive definite** by an interval Cholesky (or Hermitian LDL†) factorisation in which the interval lower bound of every pivot is strictly positive, and rejects otherwise; no other test is admitted (nonnegative leading principal minors do not imply positive semidefiniteness: diag(0, −1)).
  <1>9 [req] Producers make witnesses strictly feasible by replacing Y with Y + δI and mu with mu′ = mu + δ·(d + 1) for a small rational δ > 0 (any mu′ > mu + δ·d suffices): then Y ± J(Φ) ⪰ δI ≻ 0 and, since Tr_out(Y + δI) = Tr_out(Y) + δ·d·I, mu′·I − Tr_out(Y + δI) ⪰ δI ≻ 0; ‖Φ‖◇ ≤ mu′, which MUST be ≤ `bound`.
  <1>10 [req] That recipe assumes an exactly feasible witness.
  <1>11 [req] For a numerically obtained witness the producer first certifies lower bounds −η₊, −η₋, −η_s on the smallest eigenvalues of Y − J(Φ), Y + J(Φ) and mu·I − Tr_out(Y), chooses δ > max(η₊, η₋, η_s) and publishes Y′ = Y + δI and mu′ = mu + (d + 1)·δ: then Y′ ± J(Φ) ⪰ (δ − η±)I ≻ 0 and mu′·I − Tr_out(Y′) ⪰ (δ − η_s)I ≻ 0.
  <1>12 [req] These margins make the matrices positive definite; they do not make the interval test conclusive: every pivot enclosure the checker computes at `precision_bits` MUST still have a strictly positive lower endpoint, else the checker rejects (no fixed allowance such as 2^−`precision_bits` bounds the interval error, which depends on d and conditioning).   deps: ISA-12.1-P12.S8 (positive definite)
  <1>13 [def] **Interval arithmetic** at `precision_bits` means every operation rounds outward to `precision_bits`-bit binary rationals; transcendental entries (`rx ry rz`) are enclosed by a Taylor series with the Lagrange remainder bound (§17 item 8 fixes the term count).   deps: ISA-17-P9
  <1>14 [req] A `numerical` or `channel` subject's `schedule_hash` is required: it names the realisation the claim is about.
  <1>15 [req] K13 also requires it for `expected: "oracle"`; standalone reference relations use the explicit invocation binding instead.   deps: ISA-0.2-P13.S1 (K13)
ISA-12.1-P13
  <1>1 [req] **Sampling.** For each domain component with range size R = hi − lo + 1, assemble m = ⌈⌈log₂ R⌉/64⌉ consecutive generator words (least-significant word first) into an integer, reduce modulo 2^⌈log₂ R⌉, reject and redraw if ≥ R, add `lo`; components are consumed in enumeration order.
ISA-12.1-P14
  <1>1 [req] **Invocation identity witnesses (K63).** For `qinplace @G @G tk x` at action A's `entry:0`, the two call edges below are distinct even with identical arguments, and each prefix is also a round-trippable schedule path selecting that invocation's descendants.   deps: ~ISA-0.2-P63.S1 (K63)   declared: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11
  <1>2 [req] For two `lift.call` sites forwarding root token positions 0 and 1 into lproc f, whose `entry:0` contains one `lift.oracle_call`, the next two keys distinguish the row instances at outer step 1, even if token values happen to agree.   deps: ~ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)   declared: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-12.1-P11
  <1>3 [req] The `instance`/`call_index` fields are present in both schedule path prefixes and context keys; context-only step/phase fields below are supplied separately as §11 selectors in the schedule form.   deps: ISA-12.1-P11.S2 (context key), ~ISA-11   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-11-P5, ISA-12.1-P11
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 52 lines) {
````

### §12.2 Domains   [ISA-12.2]

````text
ISA-12.2-P1
  <1>1 [unc] `"domain": { "bundles": [ { "name", "width", "lo", "hi" } ], "borrowed": [ same ], "regions": [ { "name", "cells": "all" | [ { "index", "lo", "hi" } ] } ], "args"?: [ same as bundles ] }`, bounds inclusive, enumeration order as listed, last component fastest.   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>2 [req] `args` (inverse-pair claims only) ranges over classical arguments other than tokens (whose tuples the entry's `arg_tokens` fixes, §12.3), each read as an unsigned integer of its width (`ptr`: ptr_width; `f64`: 64, its bits).   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>3 [req] A `proof` label requires the domain to equal D; otherwise the claim is `evidence` with the covered fraction.
````

### §12.3 Schema and composition   [ISA-12.3]

````text
ISA-12.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] (code block, 11 lines) versions; bindings { object_hash, interface_hash, closure_hash (required), refer…   deps: ISA-0.2-P13.S1 (K13), ISA-0.2-P12.S1 (K12), ISA-9.4
ISA-12.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Invocation binding (K13).** An invocation is `{ args: [value, …], budget: [B,heap,stack,depth] }`, with every value resolved before replay and all four budget components explicit even when zero.   deps: ISA-0.2-P13.S1 (K13)
  <1>2 [req] `args` is in signature order: `iN` and `ptr` values are canonical **unsigned decimal strings** of exactly that bit-vector value (ptr has `ptr_width` bits); `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16 lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost); a token is its four-component tuple of canonical nonnegative decimal strings, not a process-local token id.
  <1>3 [req] Budget components are also canonical nonnegative decimal strings.   deps: ISA-2-P5.S1 (Budget)
  <1>4 [req] Signature types disambiguate encodings; widths/ranges and every tuple MUST pass §5.4 validation.   deps: ISA-5.4
  <1>5 [req] `params_hash` is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 JCS **args array**, no trailing LF (§9.2), not of the whole invocation.   deps: ISA-9.2
  <1>6 [req] Thus equal token tuples have equal parameter hashes regardless of ids, while +0 and −0 `f64` parameters differ.
ISA-12.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] Each standalone table/program/structural claim MUST carry its own `invocation`; it binds **every** classical argument and budget.
  <1>2 [req] Missing values have no implicit zero/default.
  <1>3 [req] Claims with no arguments still bind `args: []` and their budget.
  <1>4 [req] K4 inverse-pair claims instead bind the entry's budget, `arg_tokens` and quantified `args` domain as already specified; this rule does not replace that quantification by one chosen argument.   deps: ISA-0.2-P4.S1 (K4)
  <1>5 [req] A parameter-uniform structural claim MAY instead bind `invocation: { args: "all", budget: "all" }`: its checker MUST discharge the rule's side conditions for every well-typed argument vector and valid budget, not by replaying a chosen instance.
  <1>6 [req] Thus generic library and adjoint/translation proofs retain their universal scope.
  <1>7 [req] A scheduled certificate MUST carry the root `bindings.invocation`; its args hash and budget MUST equal the root schedule's bindings, and all nested arguments and budgets MUST be resolved at each realised invocation context (§12.1), including default-covered contexts.   deps: ISA-12.1
  <1>8 [req] Explicit nested bindings, if supplied, MUST equal those resolutions; a root binding MUST NOT be silently reused for a different callee.
  <1>9 [req] A checker MUST reject absent or conflicting bindings before replay, and an executor may use a verified result only for the invocation(s) it actually covers.
  <1>10 [req] Reference table/program/structural claims need no schedule; they prove only their reference relation, not a target realisation.
  <1>11 [req] Scheduled/numerical/channel claims retain the expansion library gate and all existing context and composition obligations.
ISA-12.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] **CLAIM-BINDING witness**
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"signature":["i128","f64","token"],"args":["18446744073709551617","0x8000000000…
ISA-12.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] The first argument is > 2^64; the second is binary64 negative zero; the third is a token tuple, not an id.
  <1>2 [req] Replacing negative zero with `0x0000000000000000` changes `params_hash`; replacing the token by another valid id of the same tuple does not.
ISA-12.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] `versions` is the object `{ "isa", "object", "launch", "sched", "cert", "coverage", "target" }` of the seven header versions.
  <1>2 [req] A **variant** is a nonempty string chosen by the producer (the ISA enumerates none); it is compared bytewise.
ISA-12.3-P7
  <1>1 [req] `inclusive(R, key) = local(R, key) + Σ_callees n_c × inclusive(c, key_c)` **per metric**, keyed like `cleanup` by `<variant>:<inherited_controls>` (the realised error depends on both); every `local` value is a verified common upper bound over all invocation contexts of its key in the bound root schedule (§12.1), so multiplying by n_c is sound; an `operator_norm` value converts to `diamond` by ×2 once, at the point it is first combined with a `diamond` value; `operator_norm` conclusions (T6, controlled unitary variants) require `operator_norm` bounds throughout.   deps: ISA-14.3-P6.S1 (T6), ISA-12.1
  <1>2 [req] A controlled unitary variant requires a `numerical` claim; an ε > 0 realisation compared as a channel requires a `channel` claim.
  <1>3 [req] Executable claims (`numerical`, `channel`, and `exhaustive` or `sampled` with `expected: "oracle"`, plus table/program comparisons of a realised operation) bind to `schedule_hash` through their subject.
  <1>4 [req] Standalone reference relations have only the invocation scope defined above (K13).   deps: ISA-0.2-P13.S1 (K13)
````

### §13 Coverage (coverage 0.9)   [ISA-13]

````text
ISA-13-P1
  <1>1 [unc] `{ "entries": [ { "point": <program-point id>, "source": "<dialect-spec stable id>", "classification": "lowered" | "trapped" | "shimmed", "reason", "fault_code"?, "shim_hash"?, "count" } ] }`.   deps: ISA-9.1-P2.S6 (program-point id)
````

### §14.1 Reference interpreter *(normative)*   [ISA-14.1]

````text
ISA-14.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] Executes an oracle's reference body per basis configuration: locations and cells are bit-vectors of their declared widths (no upper bound, §3.3), pseudo-locations read-only, predication is `if`, each §4.2 row is its classical function (including the inherent `inrange` check of loads and stores, the activation records of §3.7, the `alloctab` and stack arrays), `fault` and a triggered `lift.faultif` record their effective cause (§5.4) and halt, `lift. retval` halts with success and `f(x) = result_val`, the step counter counts executed rows (on budget exhaustion exactly B rows have executed), counters are unbounded integers.   deps: ISA-4.2-P29.S1 (reference body), ISA-3.6-P2.S5 (effective cause), ISA-3.3, ISA-4.2, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.4
  <1>2 [req] It **defines** F_B and the budget unit and rejects any body that executes a non-permutation gate.
ISA-14.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] For CC/R, `fault C` has the terminal-record semantics of §4.9 and the logical history of §7.4: it preserves the complete executed prefix for eligible reversal, including activations that have not returned.   deps: ISA-4.9, ISA-7.4
  <1>2 [req] This is T4's history traversal, not the Q machine's B inverse steps.   deps: ISA-14.3-P4.S1 (T4)
  <1>3 [req] In an oracle body or lproc the same spelling remains the §4.2 fault row; §3.6 and §5.2 supply its step-machine inverse and T2 prefix reversal.   deps: ISA-14.3-P2.S1 (T2), ISA-3.7-P1.S1 (oracle body), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-5.2-P3.S1 (prefix reversal), ISA-4.2, ISA-3.6, ISA-5.2
````

### §14.2 Quantum state semantics   [ISA-14.2]

````text
ISA-14.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The state of a Q region is a vector in ⊗ over live wires in allocation order (bundles little-endian).   deps: ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>2 [req] `qalloc` tensors |0⟩; `qfree` removes clean factors; `qborrow` introduces a factor in unknown state possibly entangled with a reference system R, and the region's operation must be U ⊗ I on it (§5.6); gates act by §4.4 under the active predicate; `measure` (CC) is the projective computational-basis measurement with Born probabilities, consuming the wires.   deps: ISA-5.6, ISA-4.4
````

### §14.3 Theorems   [ISA-14.3]

````text
ISA-14.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] **T1 (totality).** For every well-formed oracle, budget and x ∈ D the reference interpreter halts within B + 1 rows and F_B(x) is unique.
ISA-14.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] **T2 (prefix reversal).** For every executor whose realisation is a composition of ε = 0 permutation gates implementing §5.2 steps 1–4 with the inverse step of §3.6, on every x ∈ D every non-interface wire is clean after step 4 and borrowed bundles hold their initial values.   deps: ISA-5.2-P3.S1 (prefix reversal), ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle), ISA-5.2, ISA-3.6
ISA-14.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] **T3 (permutation theorem).** If the realised unitary V of a circuit on span(D) is a permutation matrix and the final basis tuple equals P(x) for every x ∈ D, then V|span(D) = P.
ISA-14.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] **T4 (round trip).** For every finite CC/R trace with retained run history and tapes that is eligible for reversal under §10 — a `COMPLETED` or `FAILED` run containing no `measure` frame and no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified — the history traversal of §7.4 restores the active environment, memory, allocation table, device-model state, the ledger entries of every register the run created or touched, token validity and active history (§7.4) to their entry values; audit records may remain; observed external events are not retracted.   deps: ISA-10, ISA-7.4
  <1>2 [req] If, in addition, every region call is undone by the exact inverse of its realised forward operation (ε = 0 realisations, or a realisation that is applied as its own adjoint), the quantum state of every register the run touched is restored exactly; otherwise to within the sum of the realisations' declared bounds (§12.3).   deps: ISA-12.3
ISA-14.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] **T5.** As §6.3.   deps: ISA-6.3
ISA-14.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] **T6 (executor agreement).** Two executors conforming to §3–§7 on one target with the same schedule produce on every x ∈ D the same final tuple for every oracle (exact) and, for every action with `operator_ norm` inclusive bounds e₁, e₂, unitaries within e₁ + e₂ in operator norm; for channels, diamond bounds d₁ + d₂.   deps: ISA-3, ISA-4, ISA-5, ISA-6, ISA-7
````

### §15 Conformance suites   [ISA-15]

````text
ISA-15-P1
  <1>1 [req] A | Executor | classical ISA; run history and LIFO reversal with loops, recursion, effects, segment `uncall` (impure suffix traps; nested `uncall` inside an uncalled segment; `uncall` over a `measure` traps; a segment that begins at the run's first instruction, uncalled, then whole-run reversal), `hostcall`, `qreg.*`, fault records (T4); CC `uncall` of a CC segment (§3.1); a use reached before any definition traps 4 whatever the block text order; `qreg[*]` (examples 4, 5): run-time widths 0, 2, 3, an id stored and reloaded, `qreg.from_id` of a stale, consumed or viewed id or of a register owned by another open run traps 4, a width-3 register bound to `wire[2]` or `qreg[2]` traps 4 with no effect, aliases consumed together, ids restored and never reused by reversal, run-time slice `lo` and `len`; `qreg.*` in R with whole-run reversal and segment `uncall` (a hardware-unverified free makes the segment non-uncallable); `budget.mint` valid and invalid and its reversal (example 6); the §4.9 causes — `udiv` by 0 → 5, i8 −128 `sdiv` −1 → 6, `load` at address 0 → 7, `alloc` beyond the heap → 2, `trap` → 4, `fault 1` → 1, `fault 0` → 4 — each with the pre-fault state unchanged and exactly one record; terminal R faults and FAILED-run reversal (example 8), also with `fault 2`, `fault 3` and `fault 18446744073709551615`: exact record code, no caller continuation or subsequent source effect, earlier memory/device/tape history intact until reversal   deps: ISA-14.3-P4.S1 (T4), ISA-5.5-P1.S5 (LIFO), ISA-3.1, ISA-4.9
ISA-15-P2
  <1>1 [req] B | Executor | oracle totality and prefix reversal: every fault code; B = 0; boundary halts; `lift.call`/`lift.ret` recursion with argument capture; one forward `lift.call` and one forward `lift.ret` step of a recursive call each followed by its inverse (§3.7); stack exhaustion (`sp + R_f > stack_limit`); matched `lift.oracle_uncall`, including two calls followed by two uncalls; interface regions restored; pre-entries; `lift.free` on pinned entries, and of a heap entry whose base equals a size-0 global's (the global stays live); inherent load/store checks; row indices of multi-row blocks and their `lift.label` values; `qjumpr` to an in-range interior row enters it and to an out-of-range index faults 4; `fault 0` and a triggered `lift.faultif p 0` commit `FAULT_UB_TRAP`; `fault C` as the B-th executed row reports C′, whereas a cut just before that row reports 1; subsequent HALT steps and inverse steps preserve the §5.2 outcome and clean every frame; counters at the §3.7 minimum widths and interface widths above 128 bits   deps: ISA-5.2-P3.S1 (prefix reversal), ISA-7.2-P1.S2 (interface region), ISA-3.7, ISA-5.2
ISA-15-P3
  <1>1 [req] C | Executor, Producer | T3 over full D at exhaustive widths for oracles, permutation actions, `qinplace` pairs, borrow frames; T6 agreement among VM, specialiser, transformed forms and the reference interpreter; the §16.3 examples as normative test vectors, including the stack-capacity boundary of example 2 and example 3 over its 8 basis and 16 controlled configurations with an entangled reference on the borrowed wire; `qinplace` with `ins` and `args` (example 7) over all x, y, a and both control values, scratch clean, and its adjoint (covered by the same entry); example 10's lproc token/classical/region forwarding over every x, a and byte d, arbitrary target/fault contents, and an external reference, with successful and insufficient nested budgets; coherent calls to two binding instances of one lproc, including recursive forwarding, and matched `lift.oracle_uncall` through parameters; a library (`inverse_pair_library`) claim covering every `args` value; a token argument whose tuple differs from `arg_tokens` traps 4   deps: ISA-14.3-P3.S1 (T3), ISA-14.3-P6.S1 (T6), ISA-2-P3.S1 (permutation action), ISA-5.6-P2.S1 (borrow frames), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-16.3
ISA-15-P4
  <1>1 [req] D | Executor | channel and unitary comparison for ε > 0 realisations; controlled-interference tests; checker-computed bounds; the diamond-norm SDP witness check of §12.1, including d = 2, J(Φ) = 0, Y = I₄/8, mu = bound = 3/8 (accepted: every pivot 1/8) and Y = 0, mu = 0 (rejected: pivot 0)   deps: ISA-12.1-P5.S1 (diamond-norm SDP), ISA-12.1
ISA-15-P5
  <1>1 [req] E | Target author, Executor | JCS parsing; AST evaluation; matching; scheme expansion and correctness; aggregation from event lists; timing model; imports
ISA-15-P6
  <1>1 [req] F | Producer | versioning, disjointness incl. control context; operand schema; row classes and frame sizing; index-space flattening; terminators; name resolution; permutation-only oracles; §6 transfer and windows; adjoint table; translation lemma side conditions with the four negative tests; `in`-wire use; acyclic action graphs; grammar; schemas; the §3.1 call-tier table (R → CC rejected); envelope and hashes, acyclic (inc8, zero, sum_db) and cyclic (sum, and a mutual SCC); SCC placement in the index space (mutual f, g whose member hashes order opposite to their names); empty data lines (`NAME SP LF` accepted, two spaces rejected); canonical `INT`/`FLOAT` at every literal site; const literal ranges; the region call graph with lifted calls and `qinplace`; `call` operand spelling; param role suffixes; interface-region names; local regions as call operands; the widened `in`-wire uses of §5.1; LIFO matched uncalls and an unmatched uncall rejected; the static view rule of §3.3 (a view passed through `br`, used twice, or left unconsumed is rejected); lifted-call `args` that are locations rejected; lproc header groups and `token`/`classical` suffixes (a suffix in the wrong group rejected), exact argument counts/types/region roles, missing nonempty lists and location-as-token/classical rejected, forwarding `iN ptr f64 token` without bit conversion, empty-list short form, fixed-binding closure and instance ordering (§3.7), lproc signatures including empty arrays and changed interface/closure hashes; `qreg[*]` typing, the `measure.iN` form and width checks; `budget.mint` rejected in regions; CC/R block terminators; `qinplace` signature compatibility, its short form exactly when the three lists are empty, and its §6.1 transfer (cleaning a value that depends on x after changing an `ins` wire is rejected); CC/R `fault` literal range; linking; frame-descriptor live ranges   deps: ISA-5.1-P1.S9 (region call graph), ISA-5.5-P1.S5 (LIFO), ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc), ISA-9.2-P3.S17 (closure hashes), ISA-6, ISA-3.1, ISA-5.1, ISA-3.3, ISA-3.7, ISA-6.1
ISA-15-P7

ISA-15-P8
  <1>1 [req] H | Scheduler | §6 validity; capacity; reproducibility; specificity resolution; verbatim execution; INFEASIBLE/UNSUPPORTED; invocation contexts covering default-only invocations (an action calling one region twice, one call scheduled explicitly: a claim with one witness rejects, two passing witnesses accept); a device-minted tuple binds the schedule (a schedule bound to B = 3 is not applied to an invocation minted with B = 2, which runs under the defaults)   deps: ISA-6
````

### §16.1 Lexical   [ISA-16.1]

````text
ISA-16.1-P1
  <1>1 [unc] UTF-8; LF line endings; one instruction per line; tokens separated by exactly one space; no trailing spaces; exactly one blank line before each `section` line and none elsewhere.
  <1>2 [def] `NAME := [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*`; `INT := -?(0|[1-9][0-9]*)`; `FLOAT :=` shortest round-trip decimal with `.` or exponent, finite; `STRING := "…"` with escapes `\"`, `\\`, `\n`; `HEX := ([0-9a-f]{2})*`.
  <1>3 [req] Metadata lines are RFC 8785 canonical JSON objects.
  <1>4 [req] Every integer token is an `INT` (`ptr_width`, type widths, `bounds`, literal operands, data and region sizes), so `032`, `+3` and `09` are ill-formed; every gate angle is a `FLOAT` whose spelling is the ECMAScript Number-to-String rendering that RFC 8785 uses for numbers, with `.0` appended when that rendering contains neither `.` nor `e`, and `-0.0` for negative zero (`0.7`, `1.0`, `1e+21`, `-0.0`; never `0.70`, `1.0e0` or `00.7`).   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed)
  <1>5 [req] Where §16.2 prints `SP` twice around an empty list (`regs { }`) that spelling is canonical.   deps: ISA-16.2
  <1>6 [req] A data line whose `HEX` is empty is `NAME SP LF`: its mandatory separator space is the sole exception to the no-trailing-space rule.
  <1>7 [req] The text has no comment syntax; annotations travel outside the object.
````

### §16.2 Grammar   [ISA-16.2]

````text
ISA-16.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] (code block, 22 lines) object := header NL section*   deps: ISA-4, ISA-4.8
ISA-16.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] Lproc groups have the types and roles of §3.7.   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>2 [req] A `token` suffix is permitted only in the third group and a `classical` suffix only in the fourth, each agreeing with that group's role; either suffix may be omitted.
  <1>3 [req] The first and second groups carry no suffix, and the fifth requires `in`.
  <1>4 [req] The `token` and `classical` suffixes are not permitted on other kinds of function or on block parameters or `regs` entries.
  <1>5 [req] The additional `lift.call` lists use the existing list operand grammar (§3.7).   deps: ISA-3.7
````

````text
ISA-16.3-P5

  <1>1 [def] **Example 3** (`borrow_demo`): a permutation action with a loop bound and a borrow frame established by the translation lemma (§5.6).   deps: ISA-2-P3.S1 (permutation action), ISA-5.6
  <1>2 [def] (code block, 19 lines) bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 tar…
ISA-16.3-P9
  <1>1 [req] The bounds list is `[1]` (one `qloop`, trip count 1).
  <1>2 [req] The borrow frame is the four `cx`: V = `cx x b[0]` (target in B = {b[0]}, control in X = {x}, X ∩ B = ∅), W = `cx b[0] t` (target t outside X, B and W's controls; one control in B), V⁻¹ = V reversed, W again; no `swap`.
  <1>3 [req] On each of the 8 basis values of (t, x, b[0]) the action maps (t, x, b) ↦ (t ⊕ x, x, b): V gives b ⊕ x, W gives t ⊕ b ⊕ x, V⁻¹ restores b, W gives t ⊕ x.
  <1>4 [req] Under an inherited control w (16 configurations) it maps to (t ⊕ w·x, x, b); on a borrowed wire entangled with a reference system it acts as that permutation ⊗ I.
  <1>5 [req] The map is an involution, so the adjoint (the reverse sequence `cx b[0] t; cx x b[0]; cx b[0] t; cx x b[0]`) realises the same permutation; the adjoint's frame is not of the lemma's shape and is covered by the `adjoint_table` rule (§12.1).   deps: ISA-12.1
ISA-16.3-P10
  <1>1 [def] **Example 4** (`ones`, BLK-1): a register of run-time width, its id stored to memory and reloaded, wires addressed by run-time slices.
  <1>2 [def] (code block, 38 lines) bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 tar…
ISA-16.3-P11
  <1>1 [req] Ranges: null [0, 16), heap [16, 4096), both within [0, 2^12); no globals.
  <1>2 [req] For n = 3: `q` is a fresh register R (width 3, |000⟩); `p` = 16 (the bump pointer starts at `heap_base`); `q2` is an alias of R; iterations i = 0, 1, 2 each slice [i, 1) (a `qreg[1]` view) and flip that wire; at i = 3 the loop exits; `measure.i3` finds width 3 and outcome 0b111 = 7 with probability 1.
  <1>3 [req] The run is `COMPLETED` with one `INT` result of width 3 (the byte `07`) and one measurement record (R, bits 1, 1, 1); it is not reversible (a `measure` frame).
  <1>4 [req] For n = 2 the loop flips two wires and `measure.i3` traps before measuring (width 2 ≠ 3): `FAILED`, one record `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, no measurement, R live in |11⟩; that run *is* reverse-eligible and reversal returns R to |00⟩ and releases it.
  <1>5 [req] For n = 0 the loop does not run and `measure.i3` traps likewise.
ISA-16.3-P12
  <1>1 [def] **Example 5** (`scratch`, BLK-4): quantum allocation inside an R function, used and released, then reversed.
  <1>2 [def] (code block, 34 lines) bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 tar…
ISA-16.3-P13
  <1>1 [req] The object is memoryless.
  <1>2 [req] Forward, for every n the device can provide (else `qreg.new` records `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`): R creates register Q (width n, |0…0⟩); iteration j flips wire j to |1⟩ and back to |0⟩; the `qreg.free` finds Q clean (a verifying executor verifies it); the run is `COMPLETED` with no values.
  <1>3 [req] Whole-run reversal re-creates Q clean with its id, undoes iteration n − 1 (the second flip's adjoint makes wire n − 1 |1⟩, its slice frame restores Q live, the first flip's adjoint makes it |0⟩), then every earlier iteration, and finally the `qreg.new` (Q is clean: released); the run is `REVERSED` with the register ledger as at entry, Q's id not reused.   deps: ISA-7.4-P19.S6 (Whole-run reversal), ISA-3.3-P7.S1 (register ledger)
  <1>4 [req] On hardware the free is unverified (§17 item 14) and `bennett_reverse` returns `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`.   deps: ISA-17-P15
  <1>5 [req] Under 0.8 `@scratch` is ill-formed (`qreg.*` was CC only).   deps: ISA-6.2-P1.S5 (ill-formed)
ISA-16.3-P14
  <1>1 [def] **Example 6** (`adaptive`, BLK-2): a budget minted on the device from a run-time value.

ISA-16.3-P15
  <1>1 [req] `@inc8` is example 1's oracle.
  <1>2 [req] With b = 1 the token's tuple is (3, 0, 0, 0) and, on basis inputs, t ⊕= (x + 1) mod 256 with f unchanged; with b = 0 it is (2, 0, 0, 0): t unchanged, f ⊕= `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` (example 1's results).
  <1>3 [req] Both runs are `COMPLETED` (each under the schedule bound to its tuple if the object has one, else under the §11 defaults).   deps: ISA-11
  <1>4 [req] Reversal re-applies `@inc8` with the recorded tuple (an involution), restoring t and f, then revokes the token.
  <1>5 [req] `@overdraw` asks for heap_limit 1 in a memoryless object: the mint fails `bennett_budget`'s check, the run is `FAILED` with one record `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, and no token exists.
ISA-16.3-P16
  <1>1 [def] **Example 7** (`addya`, BLK-3): a parametrised in-place update x ← (x + y + a) mod 4 from a declared inverse pair.
  <1>2 [def] (code block, 27 lines) bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 tar…
ISA-16.3-P17
  <1>1 [req] Each oracle has three rows, so F_B = ((x + y + a) mod 4, 0), resp. ((x − y − a) mod 4, 0), for B ≥ 3 and (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`) for B ≤ 2; for each (y, a) the two maps are mutually inverse bijections of [0, 4).
  <1>2 [req] The object is completed for execution by a certificate (generated with the 0.9 precheck, which first computes `object_hash`) whose `@addya` region record carries `verification.inverse_pairs` = `[{"budget":[3,0,0,0],"claim":0,"g":"<closure hash of g>","ginv":"<closure hash of ginv>"}]` and claim 0 = `{"domain":{"args":[{"hi":3,"lo":0,"name":"a","width":2}],"borrowed":[],"bundles":[{"hi":3,"lo":0,"name":"x","width":2},{"hi":3,"lo":0,"name":"y","width":2}],"regions":[]},"expected":"inverse_pair","kind":"exhaustive"}` (64 elements).   deps: ISA-9.2-P3.S13 (object_hash), ISA-9.2-P3.S10 (closure hash), ISA-2-P1.S2 (bundle)
  <1>3 [req] With it and a token whose tuple is ≥ (3, 0, 0, 0), `@run` maps every basis (x, y) and a ∈ [0, 4) to x′ = (x + y + a) mod 4, y unchanged, scratch clean: for x = 3, y = 2, a = 1, `@g` gives t = 2, the exchange gives x = 2, t = 3, and `@ginv`(2; 2, 1) = 3 clears t.
  <1>4 [req] The adjoint maps x to (x − y − a) mod 4; under a control 0 nothing changes.
  <1>5 [req] Without the certificate, or with a token of tuple (2, 0, 0, 0), `@run` traps `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.
ISA-16.3-P18
  <1>1 [def] **Example 8** (`fails`, ISA-7/ISA-8): an R fault after effects, and the reversal of the failed run.
  <1>2 [def] (code block, 26 lines) bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 tar…
ISA-16.3-P19
  <1>1 [req] `p` = 16; the store writes `07 00 00 00 00 00 00 00` at [16, 24); the clock read writes c = 0 there (`00` × 8) and sets c = 1; `fault 21` ends the run: `FAILED`, `bennett_result_nfaults` = 1, `bennett_result_fault` = 21, no values, `@main`'s `ret` not executed, [16, 24) = `00` × 8, c = 1, tape length 1.
  <1>2 [req] `bennett_reverse` (0.8: `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`) removes the fault record, undoes the clock read ([16, 24) = `07` then `00` × 7, c = 0, tape empty), the store ([16, 24) = `00` × 8), the open activation of `@work` and the call, and the allocation: the run is `REVERSED` with memory, allocation table, clock and tape at their entry values.
ISA-16.3-P20

ISA-16.3-P21
  <1>1 [req] Rows: `lift.faultif` (index 0), `lift.retval` (1); `HALT` = 2, P = 2.
  <1>2 [req] With B = 0 every x yields (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`).
  <1>3 [req] With B = 1, x = 0 yields (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`) (not halted) and x = 1 yields (0, `FAULT_UB_TRAP`).
  <1>4 [req] With B ≥ 2, x = 0 yields (0, 0) (success, payload 0) and x = 1 yields (0, `FAULT_UB_TRAP`); under 0.8, x = 1 yielded (0, 0) for every B ≥ 1, indistinguishable from success.
  <1>5 [req] A run of `@zero_cc` is `FAILED` with one record `FAULT_UB_TRAP`.
ISA-16.3-P22
  <1>1 [def] **Example 10** (`through`, lproc token/classical parameters): the oracle forwards its entry token, an `i2` argument and a mapped byte region to an lproc.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>2 [req] The lproc makes both a parametrised oracle call and a parametrised permutation-action call, passing its token as `tk` and also as a token-typed `args` operand.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>3 [req] All token uses preserve identity.
  <1>4 [req] (code block, 37 lines) bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 tar…
ISA-16.3-P23
  <1>1 [req] The outer invocation's B is independent of `tk`'s tuple.
  <1>2 [req] The sole `@worker` binding key is `[0,1,0]` (oracle argument positions 0 and 1, region position 0).
  <1>3 [req] It has five locations (`x r fr zero bad`), so R_worker = 8 cells; forwarding the three additional parameters adds no cells.
  <1>4 [req] The two outer rows precede its six rows: indices 0, 1 and 2..7, HALT = 8, P = 4.
  <1>5 [req] `@plus_db` has six rows and no lproc calls.   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>6 [req] The byte region maps at 16 in the outer oracle and 17 in the nested oracle; both mappings refer to the same passed cells, with no byte copy.
ISA-16.3-P24
  <1>1 [req] For every x, a ∈ [0,4) and byte d ∈ [0,256), an outer tuple (B ≥ 8, 0, stack_limit ≥ 8, depth_limit ≥ 1) and `tk` tuple (6,0,0,0) give F_B(x,d) = (((x + a + (d mod 4)) mod 4) XOR 1, 0).
  <1>2 [req] For arbitrary initial target t and fault f, the final values are (t XOR payload, f); x and d are unchanged and all workspace is clean.
  <1>3 [req] This permutation extends by linearity to superpositions and an entangled reference.
  <1>4 [req] With outer B = 7 the success path has not executed `lift.retval`, so the outcome is (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`); with outer stack_limit = 7 or depth_limit = 0 the first row gives (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH`).
  <1>5 [req] With `tk` tuple (5,0,0,0), the nested oracle returns fault 1; the lproc's `lift.faultif` propagates code 9 at outer step 5, so B ≥ 5 gives (0, `FAULT_CALLEE`).   deps: ISA-2-P4.S1 (lproc)
  <1>6 [req] The nested fault, stack history, parameters and locations are cleared by prefix reversal; no additional charge arises from forwarding, commit or reversal.   deps: ISA-5.2-P3.S1 (prefix reversal)
````

### §17 Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults   [ISA-17]

````text
ISA-17-P1
  <1>1 [req] Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until the item is revised.
  <1>2 [req] Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.
ISA-17-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange form.   deps: ISA-16
ISA-17-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown gate name is a load error.   deps: ISA-4.4
ISA-17-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.   deps: ISA-7.3
ISA-17-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is the oracle.   deps: ISA-14
ISA-17-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the listed functions touch them.
ISA-17-P7
  <1>1 [def] **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its controlled variant and its certificate kind.   deps: ISA-6.4
  <1>2 [req] Default: unitary cleanup (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
ISA-17-P8
  <1>1 [def] **Expansion library** (§11): the gate-level expansion below row level.   deps: ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] Default: the library `ref` published by `bennett-isa-spec` (ripple-carry adders and comparators, linear `inrange` scans, one-hot address dispatch, clean scratch released in reverse order); a schedule that names no `expansion` binds `ref` at the ISA version in the object's header, with the full-library envelope hash published for that version in §11.1.   deps: ~ISA-11.1   declared: ISA-9.1, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4
  <1>3 [req] For a 0.9 object this is exactly `{"name":"ref","version":"0.9","hash":"5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"}`, equally for omission and an explicit binding.   declared: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4, ISA-11.1
  <1>4 [req] §11.1 fixes its schema, ordinals, scratch identities, empty removable sets and deterministic decomposition algorithms.   deps: ISA-11.1   declared: ISA-11.1
  <1>5 [req] Serialization as `spec/ref-0.9-draft.json` is a separate job under b-isa-spec-8r4; publication in `ref/` follows 0.9 acceptance.
  <1>6 [req] The worked controlled-action target/schedule/certificate fixture bound to it (zsw.4) is pending; until then two consumers agree on every order fixed above gate level (§11).   deps: ISA-11
ISA-17-P9
  <1>1 [req] **Enclosure procedure for transcendental entries** (§12.1). Default: Taylor series of sin and cos to the first term whose Lagrange remainder bound is below 2^−(`precision_bits` + 2), argument reduced modulo 2π with an outward-rounded enclosure of π.   deps: ISA-12.1
ISA-17-P10
  <1>1 [req] **Default `epsilon_share`** (§8.1, §11) when a schedule assigns none.   deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] Default: the exact rational `bindings.epsilon / N`, where N is the number of gate operations in the bound-library expansion of one invocation of `bindings.region`, including every nested invocation, that have no exact native match as defined below.   declared: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2
  <1>3 [req] For this count only, an exact native match is a `gates` entry whose opcode, classes, control range and parameters cover the operation and whose `epsilon` expression is independent of `eps` and evaluates to zero; compute this test before the requested-accuracy filter, without selecting an achieved-error entry using the default share.   declared: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3
  <1>4 [req] N counts the gate operations before target scheme expansion and includes operations with explicit shares as well as omitted shares; the root accuracy is `bindings.epsilon`, not a nested region's separately requested accuracy.   declared: ISA-11-P2, ISA-11-P3, ISA-8.2, ISA-8.3
  <1>5 [req] Gates with an exact native match take a default share of zero; if N=0 all default shares are zero and no division is performed.   declared: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3
  <1>6 [req] The resolved share is represented exactly as a reduced pair (p,q) of nonnegative numerator and positive denominator, with zero represented by (0,1); it is passed as that rational value to every §8.1 comparison, expression and accuracy bucket, without rounding to a finite decimal.   deps: ISA-8.1   declared: ISA-8.1
  <1>7 [req] This is a resolved value, not an inserted `epsilon_share` field; existing decimal schedule fields and the hashed schedule bytes are unchanged by default resolution.   declared: ISA-11-P3, ISA-9.2
  <1>8 [req] Explicit shares still supply `eps` by their exact value and the §8.1 requested-versus-achieved filter and achieved-error charge still apply to every resolved share.   deps: ISA-8.1   declared: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3
  <1>9 [non] *(non-normative witness)* At `bindings.epsilon: "0.1"`, three gates lacking exact native matches give N=3 and default (1,30), even if one gate has an explicit share; a fourth, exact-native gate takes zero and is excluded from N.   declared: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2
  <1>10 [unc] With no such inexact gates N=0.   declared: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2
  <1>11 [req] Omitting expansion on a 0.9 object selects the full-library hash in item 7, not the single-qxor witness hash.   declared: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4
ISA-17-P11
  <1>1 [def] **`any`-class resolution** (§11). Default: the first class in JCS order with capacity.   deps: ISA-11
ISA-17-P12
  <1>1 [def] **Occupancy rate of a class absent from `rate`** (§8.2). Default: 1.   deps: ISA-8.2
ISA-17-P13
  <1>1 [def] **Classical step timing of CC/R instructions and effects** (§8.2). Default: `classical_step_time` each, sequential.   deps: ISA-8.2
ISA-17-P14
  <1>1 [def] **Suite sizes** (§15): the exhaustive widths of suite C and the sample counts of `sampled` claims.   deps: ISA-15
  <1>2 [def] Default: exhaustive for ≤ 16 interface bits; 2^16 samples above.
ISA-17-P15
  <1>1 [req] **Hardware handling of `qreg.free`** (§3.3). Cleanliness of an arbitrary state is not testable nondestructively.   deps: ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [req] Default: on hardware the release proceeds without a test and its frame is marked unverified, so a segment containing it cannot be uncalled (§7.4) and a run containing it is not reverse-eligible (§10); releasing a register that was not clean is a program error the hardware does not detect *(non-normative: physically its wires are then discarded as by an unrecorded measurement; this is a program error, not a trace-out operation, §10)*.   deps: ISA-7.4, ISA-10
  <1>3 [req] The same holds for `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_qreg_free` on a non-verifying executor.   deps: ISA-2-P9.S1 (non-verifying)
  <1>4 [req] A verifying executor tests and records `FAULT_UB_TRAP` on failure (§4.9), or returns `E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` from the host calls.   deps: ISA-4.9
ISA-17-P16
  <1>1 [req] **Routing and movement cost under a topology** (§8.2–§8.3, §11), deferred to 0.10.   deps: ISA-8.2, ISA-8.3, ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] Default: no routing cost is charged and no routing is inserted; a schedule that needs a non-adjacent pair is `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming this item.
  <1>3 [req] Absent topology remains all-to-all.
  <1>4 [req] A producer can supply an explicitly routed gate sequence using §4.4 `swap` gates; those gates execute and are charged normally.   deps: ISA-4.4
  <1>5 [req] The optional topology schema and adjacency validation are decided in 0.9; only automatic routing and its movement-cost model are deferred.
ISA-17-P17
  <1>1 [req] **Exhaustion of an executor's own classical resources in CC/R** (activation stack, history storage; §4.9). CC and R carry no budget (§5.4 budgets are Q-tier), so where a finite executor runs out is its own limit.   deps: ISA-4.9, ISA-5.4
  <1>2 [req] Default: a terminal fault record `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` at the instruction that cannot proceed, raised before any mutation by that instruction (§4.9; an executor that cannot guarantee this for an exhaustion inside a region call marks the run not reverse-eligible); the point is executor-defined and the suites do not test it.   deps: ISA-4.9-P1.S2 (terminal fault record), ISA-4.9
ISA-17-P18
  <1>1 [req] **Identity-idle step** (§3.6). A variant of the step in which an idle step leaves its frame at zero: forward, a = (pc ≠ HALT) into clean scratch; dispatch k < n only; rows as printed; the successor XORs for k < n and both commit exchanges under a, with no HALT successor; then `old_pc ^= HALT` under a (an active frame holds old index ⊕ HALT, never 0, since the old index is < n = HALT); dispatch uncomputed from old_pc ⊕ HALT under a; a cleared from (old_pc ≠ 0).   deps: ISA-3.6
  <1>2 [req] Inverse: a = (old_pc ≠ 0); dispatch from old_pc ⊕ HALT under a; `old_pc ^= HALT` under a; the two inverse exchanges under a; successors and rows undone as printed; dispatch uncomputed from pc under a; a cleared from (pc ≠ HALT).
  <1>3 [req] Same frame width, same F_B and the same postcondition as T2; on pc = HALT with a fresh zero frame a forward step is the identity (candidate theorem T7).   deps: ISA-14.3-P2.S1 (T2)
  <1>4 [req] Default: the printed step, whose idle frames record HALT.
  <1>5 [req] Hook: an expansion library (§11) MAY name this variant as its realisation of §3.6, and a schedule binding that library executes and charges it; making it the reference changes §3.6 and adds T7.   deps: ISA-11-P2.S3 (expansion library), ISA-11, ISA-3.6
ISA-17-P19
  <1>1 [req] **Checkpointed or pebbled oracle realisations** (§5.2, §5.7, §9.4, §11). Default: the full-history reference expansion (B frames, the library `ref`).   deps: ISA-5.2, ISA-5.7, ISA-9.4, ISA-11
  <1>2 [req] Hook: an expansion library MAY realise §5.2 by checkpoints and replay or by pebbling; it MUST list every generated allocation, copy, forward evaluation, inverse evaluation and release, every repeated evaluation included, with distinct ordinals and scratch identities, and carry a full-domain argument that the final interface tuple of §5.2 is produced with private scratch clean and borrowed correlations restored; a schedule binds its name, version and hash; every replay is executed and charged (§8.2); the frame descriptor reports the realised history (§9.4); the reference body, F_B and the budget unit are unchanged, and T2 remains stated for the reference expansion.   deps: ISA-14.3-P2.S1 (T2), ISA-11-P2.S3 (expansion library), ISA-4.2-P29.S1 (reference body), ISA-5.2, ISA-8.2, ISA-9.4
  <1>3 [non] *(non-normative)* Binary pebbling P_{j+1}(a, b) = P_j(a, c); P_j(c, b); P_j(a, c) uses 3^⌈log₂ B⌉ base evaluations and at most ⌈log₂ B⌉ intermediate configurations; no polylogarithmic-time bound is claimed.
````
